# IDs Registry

Central registry of every Pool Party visual + code artifact ID. Source of truth for traceability across Figma, Linear, code, and docs. See `02_NAMING_CONVENTION.md` for the format.

- Generated: 2026-05-29 from the Figma sweep (investor app) + the Linear Foundation backlog + the Manager Console backlog.
- Figma file: `jjOf5DL9uVEB7WBR9nGb4A` (investor app, fully designed). Manager Console is not yet designed.
- Convention: 1 logical artifact = 1 ID. Mobile + Desktop of the same screen share the ID (responsive, one `page.tsx`). States share the parent ID.
- **Design** = state of the Figma design. **Impl** = state of the code (all Backlog until built). **Linear** = the issue, when one exists.

Totals: 499 artifacts, counted as rows in the tables below rather than maintained by hand (`tests/hackathonDocs.test.ts` asserts the two agree, after this header spent months drifting: it read 251 against 405 real rows at the start of epic POO-1022). IDs never recycled; removed ones become `Removed` with history.

**Known repeated IDs, all pre-dating epic POO-1022 and none of them fixed by it.** Five IDs appear on two rows each. They are not one problem, they are three, and the distinction decides what a fix would even be:

- `PP-PROF-HOOK-002` is duplicated, and it is the only true collision: `useDisconnectLinkedAccount` and `useUploadAvatar` are different hooks that were each given the same number. One of them has to be renumbered, which means editing an `@id` header, its doc references and its Linear issue, in shipped code owned by other work. That is a change to that code, not a documentation fix, so it is disclosed here rather than done here.
- `PP-STR-LIB-005` is duplicated, `PP-STR-LIB-006` is duplicated and `PP-STR-LIB-007` is duplicated as one block: the same three artifacts under `src/lib/strategies/v2/`, described twice. A merge train landed two concurrent versions of the same rows. The later block (carrying POO-579) is the current one; the earlier is stale text, not a second artifact.
- `PP-PORT-SCR-001` is duplicated only to a row-counting regex. The second row is deliberately keyed `` `PP-PORT-SCR-001` (loader) ``, a sub-artifact of the same screen. Nothing needs renumbering; it is listed so nobody "fixes" it.

They are recorded together so a reader can tell a disclosed repeat from an undetected one, and so `tests/hackathonDocs.test.ts` fails the moment a sixth appears.

The rail's approval rows appear ([R6], one per ERC-20 leg, which `plan.steps` does not contain); a bridge row states the quote's own ETA and, once the leg broadcasts, links to it through the shared `ExplorerTxLink` on the chain it was sent on ([R2]); a row with no hash or on an unsupported chain renders NO link ([R4]); labels interpolate `{network}`/`{token}` from the values the mapper carries, which fixes a real swap row rendering its raw i18n key) | Component | Design Done | In Progress | [POO-409](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-409) | (code-only; `src/features/strategies/components/provisioning/ProvisioningPlanCard.tsx`) @rules-v2 |

| `PP-CORE-CMP-046` | ProvisioningPanel (inline pre-flight provisioning body embedded by an op modal's provision phase: renders the plan — gas-only or multi — + WalletSteps execution, then resumes the op via onDone; integration model B. POO-1041 rules v1, hackathon POO-1022: the stepper's labels, statuses and hashes now all come off ONE key-matched row list, closing a real-mode misalignment where labels came from `plan.steps` while statuses came from the rail, which expands each ERC-20 leg into an approval PLUS the leg ([R6]); and the panel consumes `PlanRailDeps.onLegBroadcast` through a new `PlanRailReporters` second argument to the `buildPlanSteps` prop, so a bridge that has broadcast but not settled is verifiable while it runs instead of only in the degraded `settling` state ([R2]). POO-1044 rules v1: a GAS-ONLY requirement skips the funding picker entirely [R1], because the gas swap is sized by the classifier off a holding already on the operation's chain and a picker would ask the user to choose between things that do not change the plan; a planner failure is now CLASSIFIED before it is rendered (the branch was reading the flow's error, always null there, so every planner failure showed the generic body regardless of the typed code); and `PROVISIONING_GAS_BLOCKED` renders the buy-crypto escape to `/deposit` with NO retry [R3], since re-planning reaches the same verdict. POO-1043 rules v1: three built-but-unmounted pieces reach a production caller here. `ProvisioningCostBreakdown` sits between the plan card and the confirm, re-quoting through the SAME `useProvisioningPlan` seam the card reads (POO-1043 [R9]); the recovery journal is minted at the confirm and closed ONLY on `flow.status === "success"`, never on a failure and never at the bridge poll ceiling, where the record is exactly what recovery needs (POO-1043 [R7]); and a materially worse re-quote is put to the user as a `role="alertdialog"` prompt during execution, with Accept / Stop here, declining aborting the leg with nothing broadcast (POO-1043 [R8]). POO-1047 rules v1: the panel mounts the shipped `PriceImpactGate` (PP-STR-CMP-022) above its confirm and blocks the CTA on it, so a funding route is gated exactly like an operation swap and the funding path stops being the way around the POO-1011 gate. ONE gate per PLAN [R4] on `planPriceImpactPct` (the cost model's worst-AMM-leg figure, i.e. the percent the breakdown already prints), live only while the route is on screen so the shipped worsen-reset [R2] applies to a re-quote unchanged; bridge legs excluded [R5] and an absent/malformed figure means NO gate [R3]. The gate is INDEPENDENT of POO-1044's gas-only branch: a gas-only plan opens straight on the plan phase with no picker, so its swap-gas leg's impact is gated too. Reaches all six operations and the `/swap` screen, which delegate to this panel) POO-1048 rules v1: the panel owns the funding funnel (PP-CORE-LIB-058) from the sources list through to exactly one terminal outcome. Completion is emitted from the flow-status effect, once every leg's `run()` has resolved, and NEVER from the confirm click [R3]; every terminal failure emits, and a session that concluded neither way emits an abandonment on unmount, so `started = completed + failed + abandoned` holds [R4]. A bridge still in flight at the poll ceiling is deliberately neither. | Component | n/a (code) | In Progress | [POO-418](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-418), [POO-1043](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1043), [POO-1044](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1044), [POO-1047](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1047), [POO-1048](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1048) | (code-only; `src/features/strategies/components/ProvisioningPanel.tsx`) @rules-v9 |

| `PP-CORE-LIB-051` | Uniswap Trading API schemas (Zod contracts for every endpoint we call: quote / check_approval / swap / plan / plan status / swappable_tokens / swaps). The quote object and its permit data are forwarded VERBATIM to `/swap` and `/plan`, so they are `passthrough` at every level, NESTED objects included (`input`, `output`, `gasInfo`), because `uniswapFetch` returns the parsed data and a non-passthrough nested object silently drops fields and surfaces as a confusing upstream 400. A live probe of the exact request it refused answers `200` with `routing: "BRIDGE"`, so it was declining a route the API serves, and provisioning is exact-output shaped by nature ("land exactly $X on the target chain"). `quote.estimatedFillTimeMs` is now declared rather than left to `passthrough`, because the planner reads it for the bridge step's ETA. | Lib | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1028](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1028), [POO-1054](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1054), [POO-1034](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1034), [POO-1022](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1022) | (code-only; `src/lib/uniswap/schemas.ts`; epic POO-1022 Universal Funding) @rules-v3 |

The INTERSECTION of the shipped multi-chain holdings read (`fetchWalletHoldings`, no second balance reader) and what Uniswap can route (`GET /swappable_tokens` scoped per held token), so a token we cannot move is never offered and each entry carries the chains it reaches. Degradation is layered, because a funded wallet rendering as empty is the worst outcome here: one chain failing is skipped, EVERY chain failing falls back to the USDC-only on-chain read, one token's routability lookup failing drops that token only. Dust (`MIN_DISPLAY_USD`, shared with the wallet modal) is filtered BEFORE the fan-out, so it never costs an upstream call; USD comes from the holdings feed, never a `/quote` per row. Balances are BASE units as decimal strings, converted from the backend's exact decimal (`TokenBalance.amountExact`, added here) and truncated rather than rounded, because a float rounds an 18-decimal balance UP and a swap sized above the real balance reverts. | `PP-CORE-LIB-054` | Gas feasibility classifier (`classifyGasFeasibility`: the chicken-and-egg modelled explicitly, since a chain can only fund an operation if it can first pay for a transaction on ITSELF). Every candidate source chain gets exactly one verdict from INJECTED quotes and balances: **OK** (native covers the quoted legs plus headroom), **TOP_UP** (above zero but short, so a `swap-gas` step is sized from the live quote), **BLOCKED** (nothing can originate here). A BLOCKED chain is RETURNED with its reason key and its two escapes (bridge native in from a chain with surplus, or buy crypto), never silently omitted: hiding the row makes the user's own funds look like they do not exist. Gas comes from `/quote` (`quoteGasUsd` reads `gasFeeUSD`, falling back to `gasInfo.gasFeeUSD`, never deriving USD from the wei `gasFee`), which is what retires the hardcoded `gasEstimateUsd: 0.5` and the five copies of `NETWORK_FEE_USD = 0.3`. The requirement carries headroom (25%, floored at $0.05 ≈ one further cheap-L2 transaction) so a plan never lands the wallet at exactly zero native. The gas-swap slice is BigInt base-unit math over an integer micro-dollar ratio, so an 18-decimal balance past `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` is not rounded; a holding that could not cover the requirement even entirely is refused rather than spent on an impossible plan. Pure (no I/O, React, viem or clock) and client-importable, so it stays on the browser side of the ADR 0003 boundary; chain names and native symbols are resolved by the UI from `src/lib/chains/config.ts`, not duplicated here. | Lib | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1032](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1032), [POO-1022](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1022) | (code-only; `src/lib/provisioning/gasFeasibility.ts`; epic POO-1022 Universal Funding) @rules-v1 |
| `PP-CORE-LIB-055` | Real provisioning planner (`buildPlan`: the engine of Universal Funding). Decomposes "this operation needs N USDC on chain X" into ordered, executable legs of routes the live Trading API actually serves. Route shape is decided by two comparisons: same-chain different-token is ONE `CLASSIC` swap, cross-chain same-token is ONE `BRIDGE` leg, and cross-chain different-token (the flagship WETH-Polygon to USDC-Arbitrum, a live `404 ResourceNotFound`) becomes swap-to-the-source-chain's-USDC THEN bridge, so the unroutable single quote is never requested. Sizing runs BACKWARDS from the amount that has to land, with `EXACT_OUTPUT` quotes (the request shape POO-1028 [R4] wrongly forbade), falling back to an `EXACT_INPUT` drain when a source cannot cover the requirement; a leg fed by a previous leg's quoted output is flagged `requoteAtExecution`, because a bridge never delivers exactly its quote and the rail must re-size from the balance that really arrived. Sources are consumed in SELECTION order, so the plan the user reviewed is the plan that executes; a `BLOCKED` or unclassified chain (POO-1032) is neither planned from nor quoted, and a `TOP_UP` chain's `swap-gas` leg is emitted BEFORE the first leg that spends from it (and rolled back if that source turns out unusable). Per-leg gas comes from the quote (`quoteGasUsd`), never a constant, and the plan's `ttlMs` is the documented quote expiry tightened by any Permit2 `sigDeadline` that expires sooner, replacing the mock's invented 60 s. All amount math is BigInt base units; USD figures are derived FROM the exact amounts and never used to compute one. `server-only` (ADR 0003): it prices through the `"use server"` Uniswap layer, and the client barrel deliberately does not re-export it. Approvals are NOT planned here, since an allowance is a property of the chain at execution time. POO-1044 rules v1 [R3]: a `BLOCKED` TARGET chain fails the plan outright with `PROVISIONING_GAS_BLOCKED`, before a single quote is spent. The operation's last step is a transaction on its own chain, so a chain that cannot pay for one cannot run the operation however well-funded the route into it is; without the guard a gas-only requirement there planned NO legs and assembled as `needed: false`, a plan card whose confirm ran nothing, reported success, and handed the operation back to a wallet that still could not broadcast. | Lib | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1034](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1034), [POO-1044](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1044), [POO-1022](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1022) | (code-only; `src/lib/provisioning/buildPlan.ts`; epic POO-1022 Universal Funding) @rules-v2 |
| `PP-CORE-LIB-056` | Provisioning cost model (`buildCostBreakdown`: what a funding plan costs, itemized per source and in aggregate, plus the four `ProvisioningQuote` figures the "You pay" line is built from). ONE pure function over the plan's own steps, run by BOTH the server-only planner (to fill its quote) and the cost table (POO-1040, to render it), so the total a user approves and the breakdown they read cannot drift apart. `totalPayUsd = shortfallUsd + bufferUsd + feesUsd` is the contract's own definition and is not re-derived: buffer is the provisioning transactions' quoted gas plus the slippage allowance, fees are the bridge's quoted spread (`amountIn - amountOutQuoted`, exact because both sides of a bridge leg are the same asset). A **swap fee line is deliberately absent**: the quoted output the route is sized against is already net of it, so charging it would double-count; the swap's realized cost is REPORTED as an informational line and is a term of no total. Slippage applies to AMM legs ONLY, because Across quotes a bridge leg and a tolerance we were never given is a fiction; a `SIGN_MSG` step contributes zero gas whatever figure rides on its quote; price impact is the quote's own, worst-leg-wins, and its absence on a bridge leg is correct rather than a gap. Legs are attributed to the holding that PAYS for them (a leg fed by the previous leg's output continues that route), so a chain's gas top-up is charged to the source that buys it instead of rendering as a second row for one token. Money: BigInt over base-unit decimal strings for the spread, integer micro-dollars for USD, rounded to cents on the way out so a breakdown a person reads adds up in IEEE-754. `bridgeFeeTooltipInput` threads the bridge fee into the canonical fee tooltip, whose Bridge line had been a "Coming soon" placeholder since POO-800 for want of a real figure, and withholds it when the quote priced no spread. Pure and client-importable: no I/O, no clock, no `server-only`. **rules v2 (POO-1047):** the model also produces the plan's ONE gating figure, `planPriceImpactPct` = the aggregate worst AMM leg, which is the number the breakdown prints and therefore the number the user is asked to acknowledge (two derivations is how a gate and a receipt come apart). A bridge leg's `priceImpactPct` is now refused at the source rather than assumed absent: `buildPlan` copies whatever the quote reports onto any leg, and an AMM impact on an Across-quoted leg describes nothing [R5]. A malformed figure still yields none, which the gate reads as NO gate [R3]. | Lib | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1035](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1035), [POO-1047](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1047), [POO-1022](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1022) | (code-only; `src/lib/provisioning/costBreakdown.ts`; epic POO-1022 Universal Funding) @rules-v2 |
| `PP-CORE-LIB-057` | Provisioning gate context (`buildProvisioningGateContext`: everything the pre-flight gate needs to know about a wallet, read once, server-side). Replaces the hard-disable stub `realProvisioningInput` had been since POO-411 - it ignored its arguments and returned a wallet holding a million dollars, so the gate could never trip and the whole rail below it was unreachable outside a local mock demo. Answers three things: **what is where** ([R1], per-chain native and token USD from the shipped multi-chain holdings read, native and token kept separate because only a chain's OWN native coin can pay for a transaction there), **can each chain transact** ([R9], a `GasFeasibility` verdict for every candidate source chain AND for the operation's own chain, which makes PP-STR-CMP-023's no-verdict-means-selectable fallback unreachable in production rather than load-bearing), and **what gas actually costs** ([R4], a live `POST /quote` rather than the `0.5` still hardcoded at `mapManagerStrategyDetail.ts:180` - the gate runs BEFORE the operation is built, and before this epic the only genuine gas figure in the codebase existed only AFTER a build). Fail-safe by design ([R6]): a degraded read resolves to NO context and no context means no gate, so a wallet that is genuinely funded is never blocked by a provisioning modal because a balance endpoint blipped. Deliberately does NOT reuse PP-CORE-LIB-053's USDC-only on-chain fallback, which carries no native balances and would therefore read every chain as out of gas precisely while the backend is unhealthy: degrading to "we do not know" is honest, degrading to "you have no gas" is a lie that costs the user their transaction. | `PP-CORE-SEC-001` | Funding-rail security review (POO-1050 rules v1, hackathon POO-1022). Three artifacts, one per gap the review found. (1) **`scripts/bundle-secrets-check.ts`** + `pnpm secrets:check` ([R1], the epic's highest-severity check): greps the BUILD OUTPUT for the VALUES of every server-only secret across `.next/static/**` plus every prerendered `.html`/`.rsc` payload (an RSC flight payload reaches the browser too), excluding `.next/server/**` JS and `.next/cache`; also fails on any `NEXT_PUBLIC_` twin of a server-only name, which is the failure ADR 0003 calls invisible (typecheck, lint, test and i18n:check all pass while the key ships to every browser). Refuses to pass with no build to scan, and never prints the value it matched. (2) **`src/features/strategies/lib/fundingAuthorisation.ts`** ([R3]/[R4]/[R6]): the rail used to broadcast `/check_approval` calldata unread, so `approve(Permit2, 1e18)` and `approve(anyone, MAX_UINT256)` were indistinguishable, and Uniswap's documented flow returns the unbounded one. It now DECODES the approval, refuses anything that is not an `approve` on this leg's own token or that carries native value, and CAPS the amount to the plan (the provider's spender is kept: `/check_approval` returns the right target per routing type, and the cap is what makes an unexpected one survivable). Capping the ERC-20 allowance bounds the whole authorisation chain, since Permit2 can only move what the token allowance to Permit2 permits. The Permit2 struct is verified before signing (verifying contract pinned to Permit2 on the LEG's chain, domain chainId matched, every `details` entry's token matched, effectively-infinite expiration refused) and its amount REPORTED rather than rewritten. (3) **`src/lib/security/fundingRailBoundary.test.ts`** ([R2]/[R5]/[R6]/[R7]): the invariants as tests, plus the two audits the issue names by hand, the journal's `localStorage` contents (no signature, calldata, permit or quote; nothing hex-shaped longer than a hash) and the cross-tab lease, whose `heartbeatAt` now falls back to `updatedAt` so a crafted record with the field missing cannot be read as free. | Lib | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1050](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1050), [POO-1022](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1022) | (code-only; `scripts/bundle-secrets-check.ts`, `src/features/strategies/lib/fundingAuthorisation.ts`, `src/lib/security/fundingRailBoundary.test.ts`; epic POO-1022 Universal Funding) @rules-v1 |

| `PP-CORE-CMP-070` | GasTopUpBody (the body of `Not enough gas`, Figma `6550:569`), POO-1509 rules v1 epic POO-1498. Subtitle, the amount control, the [R36] conversion note, the CTA and the ghost exit; the CHROME belongs to the host, which is why it is a body and not a modal. Two hosts: `BuyGasModal` (`PP-CORE-MOD-010`) inside its Sheet, and `ProvisioningPanel`'s (`PP-CORE-CMP-046`) gas-only branch inside the operation modal's dialog. **Extracted rather than reimplemented, because both halves already existed and neither was in the right place:** the designed screen sat in `BuyGasModal` with zero production consumers while the live gas-only path rendered a plan CARD with an inline picker inside `Fund this transaction`, which is exactly what [R34] forbids. A copy per host would have been a second spelling of the [R36] disclosure, and that sentence is the only thing on screen that tells a user how their gas is paid for. `source` decides the presets AND the bounds ([R35]: `$5`/`$10` swapping a holding, `$10`/`$25` on a card where the `$10` is the Paybis fiat floor); `confirmDisabled` is how the host keeps the POO-1047 price-impact acknowledgement on this CTA, because auxiliary does not mean ungated and this screen signs a real swap. **POO-1525 rules v1 (epic POO-1498):** the CTA + dismiss pair moved into `StickyActionFooter` (`PP-STR-CMP-027`), pinning state `8`'s terminal action to both hosts at once. | Component | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1509](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1509), [POO-1498](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1498), [POO-1525](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1525), [POO-1526](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1526) | (code-only; `src/features/strategies/components/provisioning/GasTopUpBody.tsx`; keys `provisioning.gas.*`, already in all 12 locales) **Provisioning v3 mobile (POO-1526 [M5.2]):** `Not now` is its own full-width row inside that footer, so it carries an explicit `min-h-11` rather than an invisible hit area. @rules-v3 |
| `PP-CORE-CMP-071` | ExecutionCarousel (the execution surface of the funding flow, Figma `7` `6550:615` / `7c` `7342:766` / `7d` `7346:766` / `7f` `7360:766` / `7i` `7381:832`), POO-1504 rules v1 epic POO-1498. **One step visible at a time** in a clipping vertical window, with the whole list one tap away ([R21]); it replaces `ProvisioningPlanCard` in `running` mode (POO-1088 [F5-R1]). The reason is P46: four step bodies at once reads as difficulty, and difficulty read at a glance is abandonment. `Step N of M` is right-aligned and quiet ([R22]) and counts the LEGS, excluding the op anchor, which the rail never runs. The progress bar sits on the BOX's top edge at `(done + 0.5) / total` ([R23]): without the half it is frozen for the whole of a 60-second bridge and empty on the first step, which are the two moments a user is deciding whether the app works. **Tense follows state** ([R24], `stepTitleKey` in `PP-CORE-LIB-060`): done reads past, running gerund, not started plain, because three rows saying `Converting` describe a machine doing three things at once. A subtitle exists only where the user must act or there is a real warning ([R25]); `Done` and `Waiting` are the mark repeated in words. `What am I signing?` is a SLOT rendered below the window ([R26]) so rows keep one height. Liveness never stops ([R57]): the spinner turns once per 1.2s and the `Processing` dots cycle at 400ms on an interval rather than a keyframe, since they are text. After 10s on the same step the box adds the taking-longer line, additively ([R58]), on a timer keyed to the step so a route of nine-second legs never shows it. The [R31] ticker (`translateY` 0 to -100% out, 100% to 0 in, 320ms `cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1)`, cross-fade under `prefers-reduced-motion`) lives in `globals.css` as `.pp-ticker-in/out`, one place for both halves, and the departing row is taken out of flow and `aria-hidden` for those 320ms. **The POO-1109 e2e contract is split rather than duplicated:** the full list stays mounted while collapsed and carries `data-testid="wallet-step-<key>"` / `data-status` / `data-tx-hash`, while the window copy carries `aria-current`. Emitting the testids from both would double every leg the harness sees. **POO-1507 rules v1 (epic POO-1498):** the `current`/`currentIndex` selection is no longer inline here; it calls `executionCopy.ts`'s `currentStepPosition`, extracted so `Step N of M` cannot drift from the same numbers the [R29] stop confirmation quotes ([D6]) — the SAME fallback chain existing in two places was exactly the drift risk. Behaviour is unchanged; a mutation test confirms the two stay identical. POO-1786 rules v1 (code header v4): `ProcessingDots` renders its label in its own span, so unmounting the dots (InvestModal's settled park, ProvisioningPanel's `runFinished`, WalletSteps' `awaitingUser`) removes an element React owns rather than a text node page translation detached (site S10 of the POO-1762 crash shape); the test replays the rewrite. | Component | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1504](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1504), [POO-1498](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1498), [POO-1568](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1568) | (code-only; `src/features/strategies/components/provisioning/ExecutionCarousel.tsx`; keys `provisioning.stepsRunning.*`, `provisioning.stepsDone.*`, `provisioning.exec.*`, `sign.stepsRunning/stepsDone.approve` in all 12 locales) **Provisioning v3 visual QA (POO-1568 [R1]):** the collapsed window's explorer-link slot is GONE from this component. It rendered between the box and the panel's pinned footer, which put a bare underlined line above the primary `Done`; the running leg's link is now the state button's own secondary action and `ProvisioningPanel` renders it inside that footer. `rowLink` survives unchanged and is now EXPANDED-LIST ONLY, so a hashed leg that settled two steps ago is still verifiable ([R2] of POO-1037). **De-duplicated on review (Rafael, 2026-08-13):** moving the link into the footer left the expanded list's copy in place, so a user who tapped the list open saw `View on explorer` TWICE against the identical transaction, once in the running leg's row and once under the state button. The footer link stays UNCONDITIONAL and the CURRENT step's row is what yields: `ProvisioningPanel`'s `rowLink` returns nothing for `currentStepRow`, the SAME binding the footer link renders from, so the list and the footer cannot disagree about which leg is current and drop a link neither is covering. Every other row is untouched, which is [R2]'s whole point. The suppression lives in the panel rather than behind a new carousel prop because the panel already owns `rowLink` and owns the footer, so one place decides where a leg's link lives. @rules-v3 |
| `PP-CORE-CMP-064` | FundingRoutePicker (screen 1 of the v2 provisioning flow, "Where from", Figma `6547:569` / `6547:627`). States what is missing, then offers the genuinely different ways to cover it, ONE TAP each and no CTA: every row is already an answer, and a confirm button under a list of answers only adds a step to a screen whose job is removing them. Renders NOTHING below two routes, enforcing POO-1082 [R2] in the component as well as in the host, because a question with one answer reads as a decision the user has to make when it is not one. Ships ahead of its trigger on purpose: with the on-ramp off (POO-1082 D3) `resolveFundingRoutes` returns a single route and the panel skips this step, so the Paybis phase (POO-1089) turns the route on rather than building the screen. **POO-1155 (epic POO-1129):** the copy is corrected ("Buy crypto", not "Buy with card"; "Use existing tokens + buy crypto") and a fourth `deposit` route offers the existing web3 deposit path as a PEER (a handoff to `/deposit`, no USD figure). **POO-1501 (v3 spec §2, rules R5-R10):** every route is now ONE LINE, verb plus amount (`Use your $215.25` · `Buy $212.00` · `Use $88.40 + buy $121.60`), the amount being `FundingRoute.sourceTargetUsd`, what must LAND, never what a card is charged. The `buy` row keeps its subtitle as the ONE deliberate exception, because that line carries the received-fixed charge NAMED by its method and deleting it to equalise row heights would re-open the POO-1153 / POO-1413 overpromise. `Recommended` marks the fewest-steps route and is ABSENT when only one card route is on screen ([R6]); `deposit` becomes a ghost LINK below the cards, outside the `<ul>` ([R7]); the composition moves behind a 16px info affordance that opens on hover, focus AND tap (POO-840 [R5]/[R2]) and is not rendered at all when no gas is needed ([R9], `1c`); an absent `sourceTargetUsd` IS the loading state and renders a skeleton of the ready layout (D1). The [R2] one-route guard lives in a hookless wrapper so a screen that renders nothing cannot report a view. **POO-1541:** the three events (`funding_route_viewed` / `_chosen` / `_abandoned`) that used to ship from this component moved to its host (`ProvisioningPanel`, PP-CORE-CMP-046), the only place that carries `flow`/`chain_id`/`strategy_id` to stamp on them; this component no longer imports `useAnalytics` or `useTrackView` at all, and `CARD_ROUTE_KINDS` is now exported so the host can compute the same card count without a second definition. **Provisioning v3 mobile (spec §11 `M6.5`), 2026-08-10:** at 375 the recommended route's `Recommended` chip moves **out of the label row and onto the button's top border**, inset 16 from the right, carrying the surface colour so the stroke reads as interrupted. Forced by measurement, not taste: label 122 + chip 95 + gap 8 = **225px in a 219px row**, so inline right-justification cannot fit. The stack above a notched button needs **≥20px of gap** or the chip collides with the route above it. | Component | [6547:569](https://www.figma.com/design/jjOf5DL9uVEB7WBR9nGb4A/Pool-Party-V2?node-id=6547-569) · [1b](https://www.figma.com/design/jjOf5DL9uVEB7WBR9nGb4A/Pool-Party-V2?node-id=6547-627) · [1c](https://www.figma.com/design/jjOf5DL9uVEB7WBR9nGb4A/Pool-Party-V2?node-id=7326-766) · [1d](https://www.figma.com/design/jjOf5DL9uVEB7WBR9nGb4A/Pool-Party-V2?node-id=7331-766) · [1e](https://www.figma.com/design/jjOf5DL9uVEB7WBR9nGb4A/Pool-Party-V2?node-id=7331-811) | In Progress | [POO-1086](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1086), [POO-1155](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1155), [POO-1501](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1501), [POO-1526](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1526), [POO-1541](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1541), [POO-1535](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1535) | (`src/features/strategies/components/provisioning/FundingRoutePicker.tsx`) **Provisioning v3 mobile (POO-1526 [M5.2]):** `Deposit from external wallet` and the bottom `Cancel` were their own rows with no icon to keep small, so each carried an explicit `min-h-11` rather than the info dot's invisible hit area (which needed no change: POO-1501 already shipped it with the POO-840 R2 pattern). POO-1571 supersedes that mechanism for the deposit row only, which now clears 44pt by structure; `Cancel` still carries the explicit `min-h-11`. **POO-1535 rules v1 (epic POO-1498, spec §11 M6.5):** **below `sm` only**, the recommended row's `Recommended` chip moves off the label row onto the card's own top border, a notch centred on the 1px stroke and filled `bg-surface` so the border reads as interrupted (measurement-forced: at 375px the label + the old inline chip + its gap totalled 225px in a 219px row, and truncating the amount is forbidden by P2). One element, two shapes: `sm:static sm:translate-y-0 sm:bg-primary/10` puts the SAME span back in the label row above `sm`, byte for byte the chip that shipped before this issue, per the issue's own "Above `sm` nothing changes" acceptance; a second element was rejected because it would duplicate `Recommended` in the accessible name. Both gaps are gated the same way: `<ul>` `gap-5 sm:gap-2` so a notch never overlaps the row above it when recommended is not the first route, and the OUTER container `gap-5 sm:gap-4` for the first-route case, where the thing above the notch is the heading. That outer one is a real defect fix, not symmetry: at `gap-4` the info dot's `after:-inset-3.5` hit area (reaching ~8px below the `h3`) and the notch (reaching 11px above the list) overlapped by ~3px, and the later-painted route button took the tap. `en` missed horizontally by luck (dot ~x149-179, chip ~x227-327); a locale with a longer heading does not. **POO-1446 / POO-1543 / POO-1571 rules v1 (murilo, 2026-08-13, from a live test-pool screenshot):** three related fixes to this screen. [POO-1446](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1446)'s own complaint (the buy option didn't name the amount) was already resolved by POO-1501's amount-in-title work; what remained was the `subtitlePending` fallback shown when no real quote has resolved, which is **reworded, not removed**: `Final charge shown at checkout`, where it used to read `Amount shown at checkout`. "Amount" is the word the TITLE already owns, so the caption restated the title instead of correcting it; "final charge" names the thing the title is NOT. Deleting the caption was proposed in review and rejected (Rafael, 2026-08-13): it would reverse the dated 2026-08-10 decision that keeps this row's subtitle as [R5]'s one deliberate exception, and it would widen `CR-CORE-016` (`BLOCKING`), which is open precisely because the charge disclosure already disappears on any quote failure, leaving the title standing alone as if it were the price. The REAL charge subtitle, once a quote resolves, is unchanged. [POO-1543](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1543)'s header-vs-route half is fixed in `ProvisioningPanel` (`PP-CORE-CMP-046`), not here. [POO-1571](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1571): the `Deposit from external wallet` ghost link gains the cards' own row structure — `px-4 py-3` padding (so its left edge lines up with them), a muted icon circle, and a trailing chevron — while staying visually borderless, backgroundless and muted-text so it reads as secondary to the money-bearing cards, per [R7]'s original intent (the fix is to the EXECUTION, not that intent). The border box is declared `border border-transparent` rather than omitted: the cards spend 1px a side on a real stroke, so a row without the box would sit its content 1px off theirs, which is the misalignment this fix exists to remove. Its touch target now clears 44pt via that same icon + padding (60px, the cards' own height), which supersedes POO-1526 [M5.2]'s MECHANISM for this row while keeping its 44pt intent; `Cancel` keeps its explicit `min-h-11`. @rules-v8 |
| `PP-CORE-CMP-065` | SigningDisclosure (the "What am I signing?" disclosure of the step a wallet is currently asking about: a collapsed link expanding into what THIS signature authorizes, with the generic "why are signatures required" copy as its fallback). **Extracted** from `WalletSteps` (PP-CORE-MOD-006) by POO-1088, which moved the provisioning execution surface off that stepper and onto `ProvisioningPlanCard` (PP-CORE-CMP-044) in `running` mode. Extracted rather than reimplemented because it is a security affordance and not chrome: it is where a user finds out whether they are granting an ALLOWANCE, which moves nothing by itself and keeps standing afterwards, or confirming a TRANSFER (UF-28 [R4], clear-vs-blind signing). A second copy of that wording is a second thing that can drift. Presentational: the caller resolves the copy, so the i18n usage scan sees real keys. | `PP-CORE-CMP-066` | PaybisWidgetFrame (the embedded Paybis on-ramp, POO-1134 epic POO-1129 rules v2): the container the widget renders into, its lifecycle chrome, and the bridge to `useOnRampSettlement` (PP-CORE-HOK-025), which reconciles the purchase from the observed balance delta ([R4]). | `PP-CORE-HOK-025` | useOnRampSettlement (the heart of the Paybis on-ramp epic, POO-1134 epic POO-1129 rules v2): embeds the widget via `PartnerExchangeWidget.openInEmbed` and reconciles the purchase from the OBSERVED wallet balance delta ([R4]), never the amount requested or the amount Paybis reports (the user can change both inside the widget). Snapshots the baseline THROUGH `useTokenBalances` (SIWE-gated, so no false-zero) and refuses to open without a session; `completed` starts a bounded, backing-off poll (ceiling ~10 min) that reads via `getWalletHoldingsAction` and treats an empty/unproven read as "keep waiting"; `rejected`/`cancelled`/`error` terminate; `closed`-with-no-terminal stays resumable; the ceiling is the POO-1037 settling phase, NOT a failure ([R5]). Publishes `requestBalanceRefresh()` exactly once per stop, never per tick (shared throttle bucket). Journals the `requestId` before opening so a reload cannot mint a second purchase ([R7], PP-CORE-LIB-067). PP-SECURITY: validates `event.origin` against a real `*.paybis.com` origin before trusting any message. Returns `{ status, deltaByToken, open, reset }`; POO-1135 sizes the downstream legs from `deltaByToken`. **POO-1403 (rules v1):** the `onramp.widget_terminal` report carries the vendor's purchase id as the INDEXED tag `pp_paybis_request_id` [R3], so the event an operator lands on for a failed purchase names the purchase and not only the state. A tag rather than a field because `extra` is stored and not indexed, which is no help to someone searching an invoice. | Hook | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1134](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1134), [POO-1129](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1129) | (code-only; `src/lib/onramp/useOnRampSettlement.ts`; epic POO-1129 fiat on-ramp) @rules-v2 |
| `PP-CORE-HOK-026` | useBuyRouteQuote (the live figure behind [R10], POO-1153 epic POO-1129 rules v3): resolves the received-fixed on-ramp quote so `FundingRoutePicker` (PP-CORE-CMP-064) can show what the card will ACTUALLY be charged before the user commits. Two hops: `getOnRampPaymentMethodsAction` lists the pair's methods (each with `displayName` + own `minAmount`), `pickDefaultPaymentMethod` chooses a card default to price and label, then `getOnRampQuoteAction` prices RECEIVED-FIXED (`direction:"receive"`, `amountTo` = the buy remainder floored at `PAYBIS_MIN_USD`; POO-1641 deleted the 1% `grossUpForOnRampFee` that used to sit over it, since we do not charge that fee). Returns `{ chargeUsd, methodLabel, methodMinUsd }`, the backend's `amountFrom` for the [R10] figure, NEVER re-derived and NEVER asserting `amountTo` equals the request. Degrades to no figure on ANY failure so the picker keeps its neutral caption: never an error, never a blocker, never a spinner. **POO-1626 retires the example that used to sit here** ("dev's sandbox has no `USDC-BASE` pair, so a production-code quote 404s"): pool-party-api's POO-1605 substitutes `USDC-BASE` -> `USDC-SEPOLIA` and `ETH-BASE` -> `ETH-SEPOLIA` at the vendor boundary whenever `PAYBIS_API` points at sandbox and restores the production codes inbound, so dev lists and prices these pairs. The failures that remain are mock mode (the host gates the hook off), a pair Paybis genuinely does not sell, and throttle / timeout / drift. Dev is exercisable up to WIDGET OPEN only: settlement watches a Base delta while a sandbox purchase lands on Sepolia, so a dev purchase never settles (POO-1627). Race-guarded like `useProvisioningPlan`. **POO-1578** split the two hops into their own effects and publishes the FULL `methods` list + the resolved `currencyCodeFrom` beside the charge, and prices the caller's `paymentMethod` when one is chosen (`pickDefaultPaymentMethod` becomes the fallback). **POO-1513** adds `pricedFor`, the (amount, method) each published charge was quoted for, because the state publishes its charge one commit behind the input that invalidated it, and a consumer doing ARITHMETIC across the pair (`/deposit`'s `minimum / charge` raise) otherwise scales a fresh order by the previous quote's ratio; and debounces the quote by 250ms, since the POST was fired per KEYSTROKE onto a per-API-key throttle shared with v1. The CLEARING of a stale figure is deliberately not debounced. **POO-1599** stops pinning the LISTING quote: `paymentMethod` is optional on Paybis' `/v2/quote` and, omitted, it prices EVERY method available for the pair, so the hook sends the pin only when the buyer has chosen one and `pickDefaultPaymentMethod` decides which returned entry is published, no longer what is asked. `pricedFor` still names the method the published charge belongs to, so `/deposit`'s raise-to-minimum guard is untouched. **POO-1596 rules v1 (F1)** stops a suppressed price from suppressing the MINIMUM: `methodLabel`, `methodMinUsd` and `methodMinCurrency` were written only inside the quote effect, so `pricingEnabled: false` (the gas-first pair) emptied all three and the pickers, which gate their minimum note on the label AND the minimum, went dark on exactly the leg that still needs it. All three belong to the METHODS list, which resolves independently, so they now fall back to it and only the CHARGE is absent when a quote fails or is suppressed. The quote still wins when it ran, because its label was resolved together with its charge while the fallback is re-evaluated every render, and overwriting it as the buyer's selection moves would print a new method's name beside the old method's figure. A contract change for BOTH consumers, `/deposit` and `ProvisioningPanel`. **POO-1618 rules v1** gives the hook the buyer's CHOSEN currency as an input and a `methodsLoading` output. The choice is a PROPOSAL: it rides to the methods action on `proposedCurrencyCodeFrom`, the server validates it against the supported set for the pair, and the echoed `currencyCodeFrom` is still what everything on screen is denominated in, so a refused choice changes nothing but what was asked. The METHODS effect keys on it, because the method SET is a function of the currency; `methodsLoading` is what stops the reload window reading as "the provider returned nothing", and it is optional in the type so the hand-built shapes in `/deposit` and the view helpers keep compiling unchanged. The QUOTE still pins what the METHODS call echoed: one resolution per flow (POO-1512), unchanged by the control. **POO-1609** deletes `/deposit`'s raise-to-minimum outright (not a guard update: there is no raise to guard any more), and **POO-1596 rules v1 (F1)** stops a suppressed price from suppressing the MINIMUM: `methodLabel`, `methodMinUsd` and `methodMinCurrency` were written only inside the quote effect, so `pricingEnabled: false` (the gas-first pair) emptied all three and the pickers, which gate their minimum note on the label AND the minimum, went dark on exactly the leg that still needs it. **POO-1612** adds `quote`, the FULL priced quote alongside the narrowed fields, verbatim and unadapted, so a picker can render every row's own charge. Its other requester was `selectBestPriceMethodIds` (PP-CORE-LIB-100), deleted by POO-1639 with the claim it computed; the field stays because three consumers arrived after it, the last being `pricedQuoteMethodsById` (PP-STR-LIB-022, POO-1666), which is how the ROWS and the MINT agree about which methods the provider actually offered. | Hook | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1153](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1153), [POO-1578](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1578), [POO-1513](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1513), [POO-1596](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1596), [POO-1599](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1599), [POO-1618](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1618), [POO-1609](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1609), [POO-1612](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1612), [POO-1129](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1129), [POO-1629](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1629) | (code-only; `src/features/strategies/hooks/useBuyRouteQuote.ts`; epic POO-1129 fiat on-ramp) @rules-v3 |
| `PP-CORE-HOK-028` | transaction flow instrumentation kit (`src/lib/analytics/txFlowKit.ts`: `useTxFlowAbandonment`, `useTxSignatureObserver`, `ANALYTICS_TX_EXITS`, `ANALYTICS_TX_STEPS`, `ANALYTICS_BLOCK_REASONS`), POO-1172 rules v1. The two cross-modal observers five money modals need, plus the two closed unions they DEFINE. Both unions were deliberately withheld from LANE-0: there was no single source to derive them from (`SigningWhy` is free i18n strings, and the exits live in five separate `Phase` machines), and a wrong union member is harder to remove later than a missing one is to add. They are derived here against the emitters that use them. **Abandonment** copies `provisioningFunnel.ts`, which had already solved the three easy mistakes: `[]` deps so the cleanup runs on the real unmount and not every re-render, the emitter reached through a ref so no stale closure reports the wrong flow, and a `concludedRef` so a completed or failed session never ALSO reports an abandonment (without it `started` stops equalling `completed + failed + abandoned`, which is the whole reason the event exists). `exitAt` is resolved as a function AT unmount, because where the user was is only knowable when they leave; returning null is how a host says a programmatic close is not an abandonment. **Signatures** observe `useWalletSignFlow`'s existing per-step statuses rather than instrumenting call sites, so a flow that grows a step gets counted without anyone remembering; fires on the `active` transition, and a retry counts as a genuine second prompt. `build` (the SERVER build, no wallet prompt) and the provisioning legs (already reported via `leg_kind`) are excluded by construction. |

| `PP-CORE-HOK-035` | Privy on-ramp adapter (`src/lib/onramp/usePrivyOnRamp.ts`: `usePrivyOnRamp`), POO-1803 rules v1, epic POO-1793. One adapter opens the fiat checkout, whichever rail is behind it: wraps `useAddFunds({destination, fiat})` with `crypto` OMITTED, which is what skips Privy's own method chooser and takes the buyer straight to the fiat flow. Its input and output name no provider, because the ADR is explicit that a second rail is a second rail and never a replacement. [R1] `defaultAsset` is ALWAYS passed and the adapter REFUSES to open without it: omitting it does not throw, Privy falls back to a `navigator.language` rule (the exact source POO-1512 [R5] forbids as a currency oracle) and the buyer sees a plausible wrong currency with no error anywhere, so the silent failure is caught here as a hard `no` with reason `missing_default_asset` and no intent minted. [R2] `fiat.source.assets` is forwarded EXACTLY as given; the probed-green set is POO-1805's to compute and a second opinion of coverage in the tree would be worse than none. [R3] the call is SYNCHRONOUS inside the click handler: MoonPay, Coinbase and Meld open a top-level popup, which a browser allows only from inside the gesture's own turn, so `openCheckout` performs no `await` before `addFunds` and the intent mint that does precede it is deliberately synchronous (`localStorage`). The mint goes FIRST regardless, so a popup that opens can never exist without a record. [R4] classification is delegated to `PP-CORE-LIB-109`. The outcome is `{attemptId, moved, reason, providerStatus?, error?}` and maps onto the intent record (`PP-CORE-LIB-107`) as `confirmed` on a provider claim, `exited` on a maybe (NOT terminal, the observation window owns it) and `failed` + `outcome: failed` on a hard no. Never throws: the caller is a click handler with no way to classify a raw SDK rejection. Deliberately does NOT resolve a settled or delivered figure (ADR-0004, that is POO-1804's), emit analytics (POO-1813 wires one emitter for both hosts), render copy, read a feature flag (the host picks the rail via POO-1800), or touch `buildOnRampSteps` / `ProvisioningOrder` / `runOnRampBuy` (host wiring is POO-1807/1808). POO-1926 [R1]: every classified outcome also ships ONE durable line to the first-party ingest (`shipClientErrorReport` -> `POST /api/client-error`, the non-error sender, because a success has no `Error` to reduce and does not belong in Sentry Issues). Three report points: the two pre-mint refusals and the classified outcome, so one attempt produces exactly one line. [R2]/[R3] the payload is ours and non-identifying (attempt id, classification, our slug, charge currency, vendor environment) and the provider's rejection text is NEVER forwarded; the breadcrumb it sits beside was never a record, since a breadcrumb only ships attached to a later event. POO-1923 [R2]: the wait on Privy's promise is BOUNDED (`PROVIDER_TIMEOUT_MS`), as a race against a sentinel rather than an `AbortSignal`, because `addFunds` accepts no signal: we stop WAITING, we do not cancel. The bound classifies as `maybe` with reason `provider_timeout` and NEVER `no` ([R5], ADR-0006 forbids rendering an inconclusive exit as a cancellation), carries no `error` field, and leaves the record non-terminal so the observation window keeps it. The SIZE is a stuck-state backstop, not an interaction timeout: a buyer legitimately spends minutes on card entry and 3DS, so a short bound would abort real purchases, and it sits inside ADR-0006's 30-minute passive ceiling so the visible phase gives up first. The timeout verdict is minted in the hook rather than in `PP-CORE-LIB-109`, because that classifier is pure over a SETTLED call. POO-1928 [R1]: that bound is DERIVED, `ONRAMP_INTENT_OPEN_WINDOW_MS - PROVIDER_TIMEOUT_MARGIN_MS` (`PP-CORE-LIB-107`'s openness window minus a named 60-second margin, so fourteen minutes), and never restated. Both were independently written `15 * 60 * 1000` while `isStillOpen` bounds an open record with a STRICT `<` against a `createdAt` stamped immediately before `addFunds`, so the instant the bound fired was the instant `findOpenOnRampIntent` stopped returning the record: the `provider_timeout` verdict landed on a record no reader would hand back, and because a `maybe` leaves `outcome: null` by design there was no age bound left that would ever reconcile it, so the record did not become wrong, it became PERMANENT (POO-1833's cross-session resume reads exactly these records). The direction is forced by the import graph rather than chosen: the adapter reads the store and the store reads nothing of the adapter's, so deriving here is acyclic and deriving there would be a cycle. [R2] the margin is sized for the TIMER firing late and not for the write (a `localStorage` read-modify-write is sub-millisecond): the provider's surface holds focus for the whole wait, so our tab is hidden and silent for exactly the period being timed, and a hidden silent tab has its already-scheduled timers clamped to roughly one wake per minute, which one full wake period bounds; a one-millisecond margin would satisfy the arithmetic and buy none of it. [R3] an invariant spec pins `PROVIDER_TIMEOUT_MS < ONRAMP_INTENT_OPEN_WINDOW_MS`, because the regression guarded is a future EDIT to either number in isolation, which no runtime test would notice, and [R4] a behavioural one drives the adapter to the bound on fake timers and asserts the verdict reaches a record `findOpenOnRampIntent` still returns, which the invariant alone never proves. NOT a retune of the fifteen minutes, POO-1928's explicit non-goal: only the ORDERING is made structural, so either number can move later without silently reopening the race | Hook | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1803](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1803), [POO-1793](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1793), [POO-1926](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1926), [POO-1923](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1923), [POO-1928](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1928) | (code-only; `src/lib/onramp/usePrivyOnRamp.ts`) @rules-v4 |

| `PP-CORE-LIB-061` | resolveFundingRoutes (pure answer to "how many genuinely different ways are there to fund this operation", which is what POO-1082 [R2] hangs on: two or more and the user is asked, one and the app just takes it, none and there is nothing to ask about. A source counts only if its chain can pay its own gas (`isSelectableVerdict`) AND it can reach the operation's chain (`reachesChain`) — the picker's own two tests, reused rather than re-derived, including their deliberate leniency: an UNCLASSIFIED chain reads as spendable and an empty routability set still allows a same-chain holding, because a degraded read must never make a funded user look broke (UF-20 [R6]). Ordering is the board's rule 5, on-ramp last. `onRampEnabled` is a PARAMETER, not an ambient flag read, so the two- and three-route shapes are testable while the crypto-only cut (POO-1082 D3) keeps it false and the step always skipped. Owns no arithmetic: the running total goes through `fundingProgress`, so the route subtitle and the picker's meter cannot disagree about the same wallet by a cent) **v3 (POO-1155, epic POO-1129):** a fourth `deposit` route kind is emitted as a PEER alongside `buy` (gated on the same `onRampEnabled`, so the crypto-only cut is unchanged); it carries no figures and the panel routes it to `/deposit`. **v5 (POO-1501, rules v1):** `recommendedRouteKind` decides which route carries `Recommended` ([R6], fewest steps: `tokens` when present, else `buy`, and NOTHING when fewer than two card routes are on screen, because a badge on the only option is decoration, not guidance); `deposit` can never carry it, said in the TYPE (`RecommendableRouteKind`) rather than only in prose, since as a ghost link ([R7]) it is not one of the cards the choice compares. **v7 (POO-1784, rules v1):** the ramp is a real option only when it can deliver the TARGET chain's stable, so `onRampEnabled` is ANDed once with `onRampCanDeliverStable` (`lib/onramp/destinations.ts`) before any route is pushed. The rail sells USDC on Base and the leg that carries a purchase onwards is a same-token bridge, so a Robinhood Chain (4663, USDG) target could only ever have landed a token the operation cannot spend: the planner has refused to emit a buy step there since POO-1779, but this resolver did not, so the panel drew `Buy`, the mixed route and the `deposit` ghost link for an operation `buildPlan` would then dead-end. The predicate is IMPORTED and shared with `buildPlan.buildOnRampSteps` rather than restated, because one rule in two places is how the picker and the thing it picks came to disagree. `tokens` is untouched (a wallet that covers the operation is not a purchase). One documented narrowing against the planner: `buildOnRampSteps` scopes its refusal to `shortfall > 0` so a gas-only ETH purchase survives on Robinhood, while this resolver has no carve-out because `requiredUsd` reaches it already summed. **v6 (POO-1755, rules v1):** a ZERO transaction is a stated target whenever the route still has gas to source, not an absent one: the five non-spending ops carry `opRequiredUsdc: 0` by definition and POO-1033 [R2] deliberately routes them into the wizard, so reading their zero as "no target" left every card's `sourceTargetUsd` undefined and the picker's D1 skeleton on screen forever with nothing in flight (production, v1.5.0 through v1.6.3). `undefined` must mean "a figure is still coming"; a figure the resolver can price NOW is always priced. **v8 (POO-1916, rules v1):** the POO-1784 suppression is REVERSED for the purchase and KEPT for the link, which is the whole point of the issue. `onRampCanDeliverStable` still gates `buy` and `tokens-plus-buy`, but it no longer means "the target stable is the one the rail sells": the bridge quotes `USDC(8453) -> USDG(4663)` at `200` (live, 2026-09-11), so a Robinhood target with a short wallet gets its buy card back instead of the picker's empty short state. `deposit` parts company from them ([R4]) and is gated on `depositServesChain` (`features/deposit/lib/depositNetworks.ts`) instead: `DEPOSIT_NETWORKS` excludes 4663 per POO-1776 [R4], so the ghost link would open a surface that cannot name the user's chain. Its old justification, "deposit has always been a peer of the buy card", is a fact about the layout and would have let someone un-gate it on the strength of the buy card alone, so the real reason now sits at the gate. Thirteen `console.error` sites across wallet-connect, SIWE, profile-save and funding-recovery wrote ONLY to the affected user's devtools console, so the failure rate of the two flows that gate every session was not observable in aggregate at all. Keeps the local `console.error(event, fields)` DX identical to the sites it replaces AND beacons a capped payload to the first-party `/api/client-error` (`sendBeacon` first, since these failures are often followed immediately by a navigation that a plain `fetch` would not survive; `keepalive` fetch as fallback). Never throws — a reporting call that throws turns a handled failure into an unhandled one. The key is spread conditionally (`...(fingerprint ? { fingerprint } : {})`) so the property is ABSENT rather than `undefined` for those 43, leaving their grouping byte-identical. Only a caller that MANUFACTURES an `Error` needs it, since every such event is constructed at one line and carries one stack. | `PP-CORE-LIB-084` | end-user client IP resolution (`onramp/clientIp.ts`: `resolveClientIp`), the value Paybis `/v3/request` requires as `userIp` and 422s on when it is wrong. `apiFetch` is server-only, so the mint is a container-to-container call carrying only `x-api-key`, content-type and trace headers; the backend's `extractClientIp` therefore found no forwarded-IP header and fell back to `req.ip`, the NEXT CONTAINER's Docker address, and every production purchase failed at the final step. Resolves from the incoming request in one order: `cloudfront-viewer-address` first (the edge writes it and a client cannot forge it, unlike `x-forwarded-for`, which the edge only APPENDS to) with its port stripped in both `IP:port` and `[IPv6]:port` form, since forwarding that header raw is ITSELF an invalid IP; then the first PUBLIC `x-forwarded-for` entry; then `x-real-ip`. Private, loopback, link-local and IPv6 ULA space is rejected rather than forwarded, which is the specific thing that prevents re-sending the container's own address. Returns null rather than a fabricated `127.0.0.1` (which the interface v1 implementation does): a made-up IP passes Paybis's format check while being a lie on a money path, so the caller refuses the mint locally as ONRAMP_INVALID_REQUEST instead. | `PP-CORE-LIB-085` | analytics error origin (`src/lib/analytics/errorOrigin.ts`: `TX_ERROR_KIND_TO_ORIGIN`, `analyticsErrorOrigin`), POO-1171 rules v1 / POO-1212 [2]. The `TxErrorKind -> AnalyticsErrorOrigin` fold, DERIVED rather than invented: `diagnostics.ts` already produces the kinds, so this is a fold over them and a `satisfies Record<TxErrorKind, …>` makes adding a kind without deciding its origin a COMPILE ERROR. Lives outside `diagnostics.ts` so the transaction layer carries no analytics concern. Six origins, not seven: `pending` was deleted as a category error (it answers "did it finish?", the others answer "who caused this?"). **Carries one stated tie-break rule:** a kind with both an app cause and a user cause maps to `app`, because `origin IN ("app","upstream")` is what wakes someone and a false `user` hides a real defect permanently. Two kinds are resolved by it, each verified in code rather than guessed: `wrongChain` (`diagnostics.ts:102-105` re-throws the wallet's declined-switch 4001 as WRONG_CHAIN on purpose, so the kind already merges both causes) and `unauthorized` (EIP-1193 4100 and `only pool manager` are the user's state, an unrefreshed session is ours). **POO-1173 (rules v1):** four origins added alongside the four new kinds. `invalidParams -> app` (the API's catalog says the code means a frontend is sending something the contract will never accept) and `staleState -> app` (the tie-break rule reaches this one: a stale view is our un-refreshed render or a genuine race, and anything excluded from the failure rate would hide a refresh bug permanently). `reverted -> app` is decided by the METRIC DEFINITION rather than by the tie-break rule: the rule arbitrates "ours or the user's", a generic revert is ours or the MARKET's, and since `market` is excluded from `origin IN ("app","upstream")` exactly as `unknown` is, reading a revert as `market` would move the most common on-chain failure between two excluded buckets for zero change to the number. A price-caused revert classifies as `slippage` before it can reach `reverted`, so "reverts on price" under `market` stays true. `noRoute -> market` is the one new kind deliberately kept OUT of the failure rate: no executable route at this size is a liquidity condition, and NOT `upstream`, which is the router being down rather than the router answering. All four are pinned at RUNTIME in `errorOrigin.test.ts`, because `satisfies Record<TxErrorKind, …>` forces an entry to EXIST and never checks its value. |

Seven operation hooks each did `await wallet.switchChain(chainId)` and trusted it; POO-1079 had already established that this is the Privy wallet-OBJECT lever while `getEthereumProvider()` returns a provider bound to the wagmi CONNECTOR, so the call resolved cleanly and left the wallet where it was, and the Permit2 prompt then opened with the strategy's EIP-712 domain against a wallet on the app default. The ladder is `useProvisioningRail`'s, extracted rather than redesigned: connector first (the lever the provider follows), wallet SDK only as fallback, never both on a success (each prompts an external wallet), cached provider invalidated on every attempt (POO-1081 pins a provider at fetch time), then `awaitChainId` polls until the wallet REPORTS the target, because a resolved switch is an acknowledgement and not a completion (POO-1077). **Bounded, which is the Ledger Live half:** a chain outside a WalletConnect session's approved namespace is not refused but DROPPED, and the unbounded await was a permanent freeze with no error to show. Silence classifies as `CHAIN_UNAVAILABLE` rather than as a generic failure. Rung 2 is skipped when rung 1 already said the chain is absent, so a wallet that lacks it does not eat a second prompt and a second timeout. Three files: `onramp/buyerCurrency.ts` (pure [R2] chain), `onramp/currencyPairs.ts` (pure [R3] supported-set parse), `onramp/resolveOnRampCurrency.ts` (server-only, gathers the three inputs). **The on-ramp asked Paybis for a hardcoded `USD` for every user in every country**, and `currencyCodeFrom` is also the key the payment-method list is fetched by (`on-ramp/payment-methods?currencyFrom=...`), so a buyer in Europe was never offered SEPA and a buyer in Brazil was never offered Pix. Reported live from Europe 2026-08-10. [R2] resolves `cloudfront-viewer-country` -> `profile.country` -> `USD`; [R3] validates the hit against `on-ramp/currency-pairs-to-buy`, an endpoint that had existed since POO-1368 with **no frontend caller at all**; [R4] any failed read degrades to USD and never blocks a purchase. Resolved INSIDE the `"use server"` boundary, because the header is only readable server-side and a caller-supplied currency would be untrusted input on a money path. **[R6] the spend-fixed `ETH-BASE` leg keeps USD**: its `amount` IS `order.fiatAmount`, a USD figure built from USD gas floors and `ethUsd`, so resolving it to EUR would charge EUR 208 where 208 was computed as dollars, and there is no FX source (POO-333). **POO-1601 rules v1: the pairs shape was wrong, and it made the whole thing inert in production.** The previous version of this row asserted that the shape follows the payload captured in the API repo's own spec (`paybis.service.spec.ts:209`) rather than its Swagger DTO, and that building against the DTO would have returned an empty set for every buyer, "i.e. shipped looking exactly like it was fixed". That is precisely what happened, from the opposite side: the DTO was RIGHT (as `docs/_integration/02_API.md` row O1 also recorded), and the "capture" is a hand-written sample of the **upstream Paybis** body, which our own controller wraps. The live response is an array of **payment-method groups**, `[{ name, displayName, pairs: [{ from, to: [{ currency, currencyCode }] }] }]`, so the schema threw an `ApiParseError` on every request, `readSupportedFiat` returned `null`, and `resolveBuyerCurrency` answered USD from its `!supported` branch **before consulting any country**. Every buyer was billed USD and offered the dollar method set (no SEPA, no Pix) for the whole of v1.5.0, through POO-1573 shipping on top of it. Three identical reports, 2026-08-10/13/14. Two further facts the fix pins: the crypto is identified by `to[].currencyCode` (`USDC-BASE`), never `to[].currency` (`USDC`): they are equal on 3347 of the recorded response's 4985 targets, so reading the wrong one fails only on network-qualified assets, which is exactly what this app buys. The supported set is the **union of `from` across ALL groups**, since `BRL` reaches it only through the Pix group and `IDR`/`VND`/`XOF` only through AstroPay. [R7] the tests now run against a recorded response committed at `src/lib/onramp/fixtures/currencyPairsToBuy.dev.capture.json` (dev/sandbox, so testnet crypto codes; the SHAPE is the contract and comes from the same controller in production), not a literal. **POO-1626: that capture is PRE-MAPPING and is no longer what dev answers.** It was recorded 2026-08-14 16:02; pool-party-api's POO-1605 landed at 19:00 the same day and restores production codes on the way out of `getCurrencyPairsToBuy`, so live dev now answers `USDC-BASE` / `ETH-BASE` where the fixture holds `USDC-SEPOLIA` / `ETH-SEPOLIA`. Checked and deliberately KEPT rather than re-recorded: the mapping rewrites string VALUES only (`sandbox-currency-map.ts` `restore()` never touches a key), so the shape the fixture exists to pin is identical either way, and a post-mapping capture would CONTAIN `USDC-BASE` and so destroy the evidence behind the [R3] exact-match test. [R5] the swallowed failure is now reported (`onramp.currency_pairs_unreadable`, with `failure` = contract-drift / upstream / unknown, the drifted zod paths, and the request id) and [R6] `pairs` has three states (`read` / `empty` / `unreadable`) instead of two, because "the read failed" and "Paybis sells this pair to nobody" produced the same USD and the same log line. **POO-1573 [R4] closes the half that was never wired**: the `source` this resolver returns was DISCARDED by both call sites, contradicting its own doc comment, so a production USD charge could not be told apart from a missing header, a failed pairs read, a pair Paybis does not sell in that currency, or a genuinely American buyer. The resolution now emits those four facts itself (`action`, `source`, `currency`, `currencyCodeTo`, `viewerCountry` present/absent, `profileCountry` present/absent, `pairs` read/empty/unreadable) so no caller can drop it again, on the same `logInfo` / `logError` split `onramp.client_ip_resolved` / `onramp.client_ip_edge_degraded` uses: a `fallback` on a request that DID carry a viewer country is a degrade and reaches Sentry. No country VALUE is logged, only presence, the posture `sendOnRampRequest` already takes on the client IP. **POO-1618 rules v1 (decided on POO-1576)** lets the BUYER choose, without relaxing the rule this file's header states: a chosen currency arrives as a PROPOSAL on its own parameter, is normalized to ISO-4217 alpha and matched against the same supported set the resolver already reads, and is discarded in favour of the resolved default when the set does not contain it (an unreadable set supports nothing, so it honours nothing). The browser proposes, the server disposes, and POO-1512's walk becomes the DEFAULT rather than the answer. It closes the hole POO-1621 parked: an explicit override used to SHORT-CIRCUIT the supported-set check entirely, which was unreachable only because the sole override was a hardcoded `"USD"`. Both outcomes are reported (`source: "buyer-override"` on the existing resolution line; `onramp.currency_override_rejected`, `logError`, when the choice is refused), which is POO-1618 defect 3: an override emitted no currency telemetry at all. **POO-1810 rules v1: the country table is now exhaustive over the profile's own list.** `COUNTRY_NAME_TO_CODE` carried 57 of the 197 names `src/lib/data/countries.ts` offers, so 140 self-declared countries (China, Taiwan and Vietnam among them, the name-bearing markets of three shipped locales) were billed in dollars by OMISSION rather than by refusal; all 197 now resolve to an alpha-2 with a currency, each code cross-checked against Node's own CLDR data in the test rather than against memory, while [R3]'s supported-set check is unchanged and remains the ONLY thing that decides whether a currency is actually sold, so a mapped currency the rail refuses still degrades to USD and now names the code it refused (`rejected` on `onramp.currency_resolution_degraded`) instead of dropping it | Lib | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1512](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1512), [POO-1573](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1573), [POO-1601](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1601), [POO-1618](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1618), [POO-1810](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1810) | (code-only; `src/lib/onramp/`, fixture `src/lib/onramp/fixtures/`) @rules-v2 |
| `PP-CORE-LIB-097` | funding source target (`components/provisioning/fundingTarget.ts`: `sourceTargetUsd`, `routeGasComponentUsd`, `BUY_ROUTE_NATIVE_RESERVE_USD`, `DEFAULT_SOURCE_BUFFER_RATE`), POO-1499 rules v1. How much a funding route must SOURCE, which is not what the operation costs. One place, because step 1's row amounts, step 2's coverage meter and `Convert what's needed`, and the `You pay` line must never disagree: three call sites computing one figure is how a meter fills to "covered" against a CTA that still refuses. Two things sit on top of the transaction. **A buffer** [R49], because selection and settlement are different moments and without it a $200 investment lands as less than $200 whenever the market moves in between; whatever it does not consume comes back to the wallet [R50], and the screen says so, since silent over-collection is indistinguishable from a hidden fee. **A gas component that differs by route** [R53], which is why `Buy` costs LESS than `Use your tokens` for one operation. **The rate is 5%, not the spec's 3%** (murilo 2026-08-10): a buffer already shipped at 5% (`SEED_BUFFER_RATE`), sized against POO-1042 [R7]'s hard constraint that the CTA must never flip from enabled to disabled underneath the user, covering max slippage 2% + Uniswap <=1% + the bridge fee; lowering the number AND re-basing it in one change would retune a live gate on two variables at once, so only the base moved, from `shortfall x 1.05 + gas` to `(transaction + gas) x 1.05`. **The tokens route's gas floor is $5, not the spec's $10** (murilo 2026-08-10): $10 is the Paybis FIAT minimum and that route swaps a holding rather than buying, a distinction POO-1084 [F1-R4] already settled in the words that matter, that forcing $10 there "would spend ten dollars of someone's balance to buy a few cents of native coin". The two routes are shaped differently on purpose: `tokens` buffers `transaction + gas` because both halves cross a market, while `buy` adds its reserve OUTSIDE the buffer because the reserve is held back from ETH the user has just received, with no conversion in between for a buffer to protect. **`gasFeasibility.ts` [R4] ("GAS COMES FROM THE QUOTE. Never a constant") is not weakened and was not amended:** neither figure is a gas COST, the only thing that rule forbids, and it exists because the app once shipped the invented `gasEstimateUsd: 0.5` and `NETWORK_FEE_USD = 0.3`. The reserve is how much native stays in the wallet, the same shape as the two consumers `onramp/config.ts` already declares legitimate; the tokens figure is a purchase floor the payment rail imposes. What a step actually spends on gas is still the quote's answer everywhere. Money runs in integer micro-dollars and rounds UP to the cent, because `(200 + 5) * 1.05` is not `215.25` in IEEE-754 and a cent rounding the wrong way is a CTA refusing a selection the meter calls complete; a malformed input returns 0 rather than NaN, which compares false against everything and would silently OPEN the CTA. **v2 (POO-1755, rules v1):** a ZERO transaction is NOT malformed: it is the five non-spending ops' legitimate cost, and such a route still prices its GAS COMPONENT alone (tokens: `gas x 1.05`; buy: floored at the Paybis minimum, the smallest order the rail sells); only nothing-at-all (zero transaction AND no gas component) still returns 0, unfloored. Treating the zero as malformed held the route picker's D1 skeleton on screen forever for every non-spending op classified `multi`. Carries the D9 seam: the buffer rate is the SERVER's to set alongside the quote, so it can be tuned per network and per market without a deploy, and a `NEXT_PUBLIC_` env could not serve that purpose because it is baked at build time | Lib | n/a (code) | In Progress | [POO-1499](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1499), [POO-1755](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1755) | (code-only; `src/features/strategies/components/provisioning/fundingTarget.ts`) @rules-v2 |

So a European was quoted in dollars and offered the US method set (`currencyFrom` is the key the method list is fetched by, so SEPA/iDEAL/Bancontact were never on offer), reported live 2026-08-13 after the fix was in prod. | `PP-CORE-LIB-102` | Paybis widget capture (`src/lib/onramp/paybisCapture.ts`: `startPaybisCapture` / `capturePaybisMessage` / `stopPaybisCapture`), POO-1598 rules v1 (S3 + S4), epic POO-1129. Records the Paybis widget's `postMessage` stream as an ORDERED, allow-list-redacted, retrievable sequence, so one real production purchase becomes the fixture set that answers the vendor questions no amount of reading can (the docs say the method list "may vary based on the user's geographical location" and never say what that keys on; dev runs Paybis SANDBOX, which sells almost nothing, so the questions are not answerable there at any price). **Why it is not the breadcrumb trail** (`PP-CORE-LIB-090`): breadcrumbs attach only to a Sentry event that is actually SENT, so a purchase that SUCCEEDS - the exact run this exists to record - leaves none anywhere, and they are read one incident at a time rather than as a sequence with timings. The two modules share the ENVELOPE DECODER (`describePaybisMessage`, exported from PP-CORE-LIB-090 by this issue) because reading the wrong envelope layer is the bug that shipped four times (POO-1368/1369/1373/1377), and share nothing else. **Where a capture lands:** the existing first-party rail, `shipClientErrorReport` -> `POST /api/client-error` -> `logWarn` -> container stdout AND Sentry Logs. No new endpoint and no new transport: that rail already survives the page unload that ends a checkout (`sendBeacon`, `keepalive` fallback), already writes one flat JSON line per record, and is already hardened (body cap, `Sec-Fetch-Site`, field allow-list, per-field clip, never a throw). Retrieval is `grep '"event":"onramp.paybis_capture"'` sorted by `seq`, or `reference:<traceId>` in Sentry Logs after a recreate has rotated stdout away (POO-1354). **Redaction is an ALLOW-LIST, the opposite of PP-CORE-LIB-090's denylist, and deliberately so**: the output is destined for a fixture committed to a repo with a public mirror, and a denylist silently ships the next field Paybis adds. Only structured non-free-text keys are recorded by value (lifecycle state, currency, amount, method, vendor ids, codes, timestamps, chain); every other key is recorded by NAME ONLY under `droppedKeys`, which keeps the whole discovery value with none of the leak risk; FREE TEXT IS NOT RECORDED AT ALL, because `parsePaybisWidgetReason` (POO-1390) does not mask NAMES and accepted that only on the grounds that its output reaches a private staff ticket, which a committed fixture in a public mirror is not (the machine-readable `code`/`errorCode`/`errorType`/`statusCode` are kept instead, the prose key is still named under `droppedKeys`, and the human-readable reason still reaches support via `onramp.widget_terminal` -> `vendorReason`); every vendor-controlled label (`step`, `namespace`, `droppedKeys`) runs through `redactSecrets` like the kept values do; a nested object or array is recorded by SHAPE, never content. `country`/`countryCode` are allow-listed as a stated judgement call, because they are the exact geofencing discriminator POO-1597 / POO-1426 Q1 exist to measure (`CR-CORE-027`). Never captured: email, name, document or card numbers, and the end-user IP. **Correlation ([R2]/[R3])**: every record carries `browserTraceId()` as `reference` (the same id `apiFetch` sends as `x-request-id`, which the API's own Paybis capture stamps) plus the Paybis `requestId`, so three recordings join on one key; where the trace id is genuinely absent the record says `traceSource: "unavailable"` rather than minting a substitute that would fragment the capture while looking complete. **Off unless deliberately enabled** ([R1], flag `onRampCapture`, read ONCE per session at arm time so a mid-purchase flip cannot split a capture) and **never a gate** ([R6]): wrapped at the module AND at the caller (`observe` in `PP-CORE-HOK-025`), because only the caller knows a charged purchase is downstream of it. Bounded at 200 message records per session, with a session-end record carrying the OBSERVED count so a truncated sequence is distinguishable from a lossy one; that record fires from the hook's `detach()` AND from `pagehide`, because a buyer who abandons by closing the tab reaches none of `detach()`'s paths (a bfcache `pagehide` is deliberately not treated as a close, since the page may be restored mid-checkout).
