| `PP-CORE-LIB-106` | On-ramp vendor vocabulary (POO-1801 rules v1, epic POO-1793). FOUR small modules replacing vendor-shaped values at exactly one boundary, grouped on one id the way `PP-CORE-SEC-001` groups its three. **(1) `src/lib/chains/caip2.ts`** (`toCaip2` / `fromCaip2`, [R1]): `eip155:<id>`, REGISTRY-CHECKED on both sides against `supportedChainMetas`, because building the string is one template literal and refusing `eip155:1` (a real, famous chain no balance of ours lives on) is the part worth a module; a CAIP-2 string arriving from outside gets the same check, or the vendor gets to tell us which chain we are on. Decimal references only, so `0x2105` and `08453` are refused rather than normalized: two spellings of one chain is how an equality check quietly starts failing. **(2) `src/lib/onramp/destinations.ts`** (`usdcDestination`, [R2]): `{chain, asset}` with the address READ from the registry entry's `usdc.address`, never a second literal, and the test greps the module for any `0x...` to keep it that way. Its header carries the SILENT FLIP: `destination.asset` was a token SYMBOL at 3.29.2 and is a token ADDRESS from 3.33.0, both typed `string`, so code written against the old shape keeps compiling and starts sending `"USDC"` where a `0x...` belongs. Base-only is a CALLER decision (D8); the function stays generic over the registry, but NOT over the asset: the registry's `usdc` field is the STABLE slot and on Robinhood Chain (4663) it holds USDG, so a meta whose `usdc.symbol` is not `USDC` answers `undefined` rather than naming a token nobody bought. **v2 (POO-1784, rules v1):** `onRampCanDeliverStable(chainId)` joins it as its BOOLEAN SHADOW, composed with it rather than restated beside it. It was a private function of `buildPlan.ts` (POO-1779), which is exactly why the panel's route picker shipped without it; it lives here now so the planner and the picker share one answer, and composing also fixes the pre-1784 reading of an UNSUPPORTED chain, which `stableSymbol` degraded to "USDC" and so compared equal to Base's own. **(3) `src/lib/onramp/limits.ts`** ([R3]): `ON_RAMP_FLOOR_USD` re-exports `PAYBIS_MIN_USD` rather than restating `10`, because this app already carries four independent $10 floors plus a hardcoded "as little as $10" in 12 locales and raising one alone was reverted (POO-1670); the floor is declared OURS (D9), so it survives the vendor that happened to be behind it. `STRIPE_SANDBOX_MAX_USD = 200` is named as the SANDBOX's ceiling and is UNVERIFIED against a shipped artefact (see its integration-point row). **(4) `src/lib/onramp/fiatCurrencies.ts`** (`toPrivyFiat`, [R4]): uppercase to lowercase with a membership check over 49 codes copied from `@privy-io/react-auth` `dist/dts/index.d.ts`; unknown or malformed input answers `undefined`, NEVER a default. Not imported as a type, and NOT because of the version: `SupportedFiatCurrency` is DECLARED but never EXPORTED from the package entry, at the installed 3.29.2 (`:570`) and at the target 3.40.0 (`:576`) alike, so no `import type` resolves at either and the copy is permanent until Privy exports the union. A parity test reads the installed `.d.ts` AS SOURCE TEXT and asserts set equality, so a bump that adds or drops a currency reds a gate rather than a buyer's checkout. **v3 (POO-1916, rules v1):** `onRampCanDeliverStable` STOPS being that shadow. POO-1784 composed it from `usdcDestination` on the premise that the leg carrying a purchase onwards is a same-token bridge, so the only stable a card could land was the one the rail sells; a live probe says `USDC(8453) -> USDG(4663)` answers `200` with `routing: "BRIDGE"` (2026-09-11, re-probed 2026-09-12: 10.000000 USDC in, ~9.95 USDG out, about 0.48%). Where the RAMP delivers and what the BRIDGE lands on the target are two different questions, and fusing them suppressed a working fiat route to Robinhood Chain for two issues. `usdcDestination` is UNCHANGED and still refuses a non-USDC chain, because the rail genuinely sells USDC and nothing else; the predicate now answers only the STATIC half (does the registry name a stable there), naming no chain id and no ticker, and `buildPlan.buildOnRampSteps` asks the LIVE half against `/quote` ([R3]). | Lib | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1801](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1801), [POO-1793](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1793), [POO-1784](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1784), [POO-1916](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1916) | (code-only; `src/lib/chains/caip2.ts`, `src/lib/onramp/{destinations,limits,fiatCurrencies}.ts`) @rules-v3 |
| `PP-CORE-LIB-107` | On-ramp intent record (`src/lib/onramp/onRampIntent.ts`: `mintOnRampIntent` / `readOnRampIntents` / `updateOnRampIntent` / `recordOnRampDelivery` / `findOpenOnRampIntent`), POO-1802 rules v1, epic POO-1793, implements ADR-0007. The client-persisted record of a fiat purchase attempt, keyed by an attempt id WE mint (`crypto.randomUUID` with guarded fallbacks) before the provider's modal opens. It exists because Privy returns no identifier on any path (`AddFundsResult` is `{method, status}`, the status endpoint carries no amount, there is no server-side REST route for the card rail, and `reference_id` exists only on actions we initiate) and its concurrency guard is TAB-SCOPED, so it survives neither a reload, nor a second tab, nor a buyer returning tomorrow. It is not a copy of anything the provider holds; it is the only place the attempt exists. Records the requested amount, the prefill we actually passed (these differ by the buffer), the delivered amount once the delta lands (`delivered.amountRaw`, base units, is the authority; `delivered.amount` is its display companion, and POO-1806/1813 read the raw one), the CAIP-2 destination chain and token address, the ADR-0006 `phase`, the `outcome` and the trace id, which is what makes three currently unanswerable questions answerable: did the buyer retype the amount inside the provider's modal, what did the spread actually cost, and where did a failed attempt stop. `delivered` is the ONLY figure any screen may print (ADR-0004: the on-chain delta is the sole authority on a funded amount), and `null` there means "we do not know", never zero. Two phase names are narrower than they look: `cancelled` is reserved for a provider-reported PRE-CHARGE cancellation, the one path on which a screen may say "cancelled" at all, and `unverified` is ADR-0006's PASSIVE-window terminal, which claims only that we never saw a delta. Key `pp.onramp.intent.v1`, `schemaVersion: 1`, retention 30 days / 20 records as v1 defaults set by the epic (the journal's 24h / 3 was sized for a rail that resolved in minutes; a card purchase takes hours and the support question arrives days later). Terminal records are NOT deleted on completion: they are the support reference. KEYED PER WALLET: the record carries the lowercased SIWE-session address it was minted for, and `findOpenOnRampIntent(wallet, now)` filters on it, because a browser profile is not a wallet (`WalletSwitchGuard` exists for A-to-B switches inside one session, and wagmi already persists the connected address in the same profile); `readOnRampIntents` stays unscoped because it is the pruning read and the support view. It also carries `baselineRaw` (the destination asset's balance in base units as the host read it BEFORE the checkout opened) and `decimals`, so ADR-0006's passive window can be resumed from the record alone after a reload. Openness is a SEPARATE window from retention (`ONRAMP_INTENT_OPEN_WINDOW_MS`, 15 min, taken from the journal's `ONRAMP_REQUEST_RESUMABLE_MS`, POO-1384's fix): a never-confirmed intent stops being offered once it ages out, while one that reached `confirmed` (stamped `confirmedAt`, first observation wins) has no age bound at all, because a card can take hours. `updateOnRampIntent` cannot write `outcome: "settled"`, in the type and at runtime; only `recordOnRampDelivery` may. Writes re-read and merge by `attemptId`, so a second tab's record survives. Holds no card data, which never reaches us. Zod-parsed on every read with `.finite()` bounds, and a parse failure discards the WHOLE store, degrading to "no open intent", the safe direction: the `-Infinity` incident that pinned an intent as live forever is recorded on `PP-CORE-LIB-067`. Compliance: `CR-CORE-035`. Ships with no consumer; the adapter mints (POO-1803), the watcher updates (POO-1804), the hosts reconcile (POO-1807/1808). The Privy-era successor of `PP-CORE-LIB-067`, which keeps serving the live Paybis rail untouched until POO-1809 retires it. POO-1928: `ONRAMP_INTENT_OPEN_WINDOW_MS` now has a DEPENDENT, and the note on the constant says so. `PROVIDER_TIMEOUT_MS` (`PP-CORE-HOK-035`) is derived as this window minus a named margin, so the adapter's stuck-state backstop always fires while the record it is about to write is still OPEN by `isStillOpen`'s strict `<`; before that both were spelled `15 * 60 * 1000` independently and the two instants coincided, which wrote a `provider_timeout` verdict onto a record `findOpenOnRampIntent` had stopped returning in the same turn. Moving this number moves that one with it, which is the point. The derivation lives in the adapter, not here, because the import is one-way (the adapter reads this module and this module reads nothing of the adapter's), so nothing in this module changed but the note: the behaviour and the rules are still POO-1802's | Lib | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1802](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1802), [POO-1793](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1793) | (code-only; `src/lib/onramp/onRampIntent.ts`) @rules-v1 |
| `PP-CORE-LIB-108` | On-ramp coverage probe (`src/lib/onramp/coverageProbe.ts`, POO-1805 rules v2, epic POO-1793). We ASK whether anyone will sell instead of guessing from a table: the quotes response IS the per-country payment-method list (one quote is one selectable method) and the route has nowhere to put a country, so coverage cannot be answered offline. Traced out of the shipped bundles rather than a guide: `PUT /api/v1/onramp/fiat/quotes` (`@privy-io/routes@0.2.12`) against `https://auth.privy.io` (`@privy-io/js-sdk-core@0.73.0`), body `{source:{asset,amount},destination:{asset,chain,address},environment}` from the `getQuotes` call site in `@privy-io/react-auth@3.40.0` `dist/esm/index-rkoxGjIC.mjs`, and a fresh 20s abort signal per attempt because the SDK's own is `signal:er(2e4)` and a reused one would abort the retry instantly. Two traps are pinned by the [R4] contract test: the method is **PUT**, not POST, and `source.asset` is **uppercase** ISO 4217 (`api-types@0.20.0` `onramps.d.ts:121-123`) while the `SupportedFiatCurrency` union POO-1801 copies is lowercase, both typed `string` so neither would fail to compile. **[R1] a failed call is not "no coverage"**: the SDK's own flow catches everything into `localQuotes: []`, so this module uses `fetch` directly to see status codes, retries ONCE, and answers `unknown` rather than `uncovered` on a second failure, on contract drift, on 401/403 (`unauthenticated`) or on a token getter that rejects (`token-failed`, distinct on purpose). **Rules v2 goes further: an empty quote list is THREE observations, not one.** The vendor's own reading is `ul=(e,t)=>e.length>0?null:3>parseFloat(t)?"amount_too_low":"provider_errors"` (react-auth@3.40.0 `index-rkoxGjIC.mjs`), so even Privy does not read its own empty list as "nobody sells": `uncovered` now requires a valid body AND no `provider_errors` (`onramps.d.ts:152-159`, `:248`) AND an amount at or above the rail's display floor of 3, an empty list beside `provider_errors` is `unknown`/`upstream`, and a below-floor amount is its own `amount-too-low` answer, in the vendor's own precedence. **[R2]** 429 is retried, which `js-sdk-core`'s own list `[408,409,425,500,502,503,504]` deliberately excludes because a global error handler would amplify a throttle across every tab, a tradeoff that does not apply to a probe fired once per buyer decision; the throttle wait honours `Retry-After` clamped to 5s and is otherwise longer than the fault wait. **[R3]** it resolves no currency: the server chain stays the producer. One live sandbox call is owed before POO-1807/POO-1808 adopt it, since a bundle pins what we SEND and not what the server accepts from a caller that is not the SDK. | Lib | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1805](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1805), [POO-1793](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1793) | (code-only; `src/lib/onramp/coverageProbe.ts`, `src/lib/onramp/coverageProbe.test.ts`) @rules-v2 |
| `PP-CORE-LIB-109` | Add-funds outcome classifier (`src/lib/onramp/classifyAddFundsOutcome.ts`: `classifyAddFundsOutcome`, `HARD_NO_MESSAGES`), POO-1803 rules v1 [R4], epic POO-1793. A PURE function, no React, that answers one question about a settled `addFunds` call: **could money have moved?**, with three answers, `no` / `maybe` / `confirmed`. Only a hard `no` may render a cancellation (ADR-0006). `useAddFunds` rejects with a plain `Error` carrying a human-readable message, with no code, no class and no discriminant, so a literal-message table is the only thing the SDK gives us to classify on; the nine hard-no strings were GREPPED from the shipped 3.40.0 bundle and each carries its file and byte offset in the module header (seven are the pre-flight guards in `addFunds`'s own body at `index-rkoxGjIC.mjs` 401029 to 401564, two are popup-open failures). That table is fragile by nature, since Privy can reword any string in a patch release, and the FAIL-SAFE is what makes it safe: a message not in the table is `maybe`, never `no`, so a reworded string degrades into an observation window rather than a cancellation over a charged card. Matching is EXACT, not substring. `User exited flow` is deliberately `maybe`: the bundle's close handler makes it the FALL-THROUGH when the state is neither `provider-success` nor `provider-confirming`, so a buyer whose card is being charged in the popup lands on the same branch as one who abandoned at the amount screen, which is the situation ADR-0006 exists for. `User cancelled funding` is also deliberately excluded from the hard-no table, because the SDK's error handler routes back to the method chooser after a failed fiat attempt, so a cancel there can follow an attempt that already opened. Both provider statuses, `confirmed` and `submitted`, map to `confirmed`: they are the provider's CLAIM about its own progress, never settlement (ADR-0004) | Lib | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1803](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1803), [POO-1793](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1793) | (code-only; `src/lib/onramp/classifyAddFundsOutcome.ts`) @rules-v1 |
| `PP-CORE-LIB-110` | On-ramp settlement observation window (`src/lib/onramp/awaitOnRampSettlement.ts`: `watchOnRampSettlementVisible` / `resumeOnRampObservation`), POO-1804 rules v1, epic POO-1793, implements ADR-0004 and ADR-0006. The settlement loop leaves React, following `awaitBridgeSettlement` (`PP-STR-LIB-018`) as the precedent: a pure async watcher, deps-injected (`readBalance`, `sleep`, `now`), bounded, backing off, honest at the ceiling, and testable under fake timers. [R1] the test is `balanceOf minus baseline > 0` on the destination, and the OBSERVED figure is what is reported, never the requested or prefilled one; the baseline is read by the HOST before the checkout opens and passed in, because a baseline taken after the buyer may already have paid would subtract their own money from the delta. There is deliberately NO floor, unlike the bridge watcher: a fiat provider quotes nothing it will deliver, every input it takes is spend-fixed, the buyer can retype inside the provider's modal and the spread is the provider's, so ANY positive delta is the delivery. A failed `readBalance` is a retry, never a zero and never a delta. [R2] two phases, because the populations are asymmetric: a VISIBLE window (`ONRAMP_VISIBLE_WINDOW_MS`, 90s, short backoff) that a screen waits on, then a PASSIVE one (`ONRAMP_PASSIVE_CEILING_MS`, 30 min counted from the record's `createdAt` and CLIPPED by whatever the attempt has already spent, so resuming never grants a fresh budget and a spent one reports the ceiling without opening a window, longer backoff) resumed WITHIN THE SAME PAGE SESSION by the host that still holds the pre-purchase baseline; abandonment is common and cheap to get wrong, a lost charge is rare and expensive. Both v1 defaults, named and flagged for Murilo. The passive phase is deliberately NOT a cross-session resume: the intent record persists none of `baseline`, `decimals` or `moved`, and a host returning after a reload can only read a balance taken AFTER the purchase, which is the one baseline [R1] refuses, so a returning buyer is reconciled by hand from the record. Marked `PP-DEBT(SEV:HIGH)` in the header: ADR-0006's passive half does not ship cross-session until POO-1833 persists `baseline` and `decimals` on the record, derives `moved` from its phase, and resumes on mount against `findOpenOnRampIntent()`. [R3] the old single `timed-out` splits in two: `settling` when we hold the provider's CLAIM (proof of payment) and `unverified` when we do not, which asserts only that we never saw a delta and not that nothing was charged. [R4] there is NO `cancelled` value, branch or string anywhere in the module, asserted exhaustively by running every path and comparing the outcome set. [R5] scoped to ONE `{chain, asset}`, so a single `balanceOf` replaces the three-network fan-out and its false-zero baseline trap. Writes the intent record (`PP-CORE-LIB-107`): `recordOnRampDelivery` on settlement; `settling` / `unverified` with `outcome` still NULL at the visible ceiling, because the passive phase keeps observing; closed `unverified` at the passive ceiling ONLY with no claim, since a claimed record stays open for support. Every ceiling write READS the record first and skips when `outcome !== null` or `phase === "settled"`, so a second tab's slower window cannot downgrade a record another observer already settled. Each outcome also carries `polls` / `waitedMs` / `lastError` (optional, mirroring `awaitBridgeSettlement`), so a ceiling reached against a dead RPC is distinguishable from one reached honestly. The delivered figure a screen prints is the STRING in `OnRampDelivered.amount`; the record's numeric `delivered.amount` is display and support only. A new module, not a rewrite: `useOnRampSettlement.ts` (`PP-CORE-HOK-025`) keeps serving the live Paybis rail until POO-1809 retires it. No host wiring; POO-1807/1808 bind `readBalance` | Lib | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1804](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1804), [POO-1793](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1793) | (code-only; `src/lib/onramp/awaitOnRampSettlement.ts`) @rules-v1 |

ONE emitter for the fiat purchase, shared by the two hosts of the PRIVY rail. The four `funding_buy_*` names were specced in POO-1178 against Paybis vocabulary and then never emitted: they sat in `DEAD_EVENT_ALLOWLIST` for five months, which is the honest bookkeeping of a promise nobody kept, and this keeps it once. The adapter (`PP-CORE-HOK-035`) reports `started`, `submitted` and `failed`, because it is the only place that knows the checkout was asked to open and what the provider claimed; the two hosts (`PP-DEP-SCR-001`, `PP-STR-CMP-029`) report `settled`, because the adapter never sees a balance. The Paybis rail is deliberately NOT wired to these names and keeps its own events until POO-1809 retires it, so `rail` carries one value today and exists so the day a second rail emits, the two are separable rather than averaged. **[R4]** `settled` fires on the OBSERVED delta and never on the promise resolving, and carries the figures POO-1811's purchase half needs (`requested_usd`, `prefill_usd`, `delivered_usd`), which closes that half; `prefill_usd` is OMITTED rather than zeroed when we prefilled nothing, because a zero enters the buffer series as a figure nobody asked for. **[R1]** `ONRAMP_REASON_CODES` maps every classifier reason 1:1 to a code, never folded (`popup_blocked` and `not_authenticated` call for opposite fixes), with an `ONRAMP_UNMAPPED` fallback so a provider adding a rejection message cannot break a purchase through the analytics path, and `onRampErrorCode` is applied at BOTH hosts so a lowercase slug never reaches the shape gate that would drop it. `attempt_id` is OUR intent id and never a provider reference. Its sibling suite `errorCodeUnion.test.ts` pins `ANALYTICS_ERROR_CODES` in both directions: no reachable `ONRAMP_*` unlisted, and no listed one that nothing produces (the five template-minted Paybis codes are exempted by name, and the exemption is itself pinned to the template that mints them). | `PP-CORE-SEC-003` | On-ramp method-icon proxy (POO-1643 rules v1, epic POO-1129). Paybis' payment-method logos are served from OUR origin so the buyer's browser never reaches the vendor's CDN. POO-1603 stopped the methods list discarding `icon`, which made the logo renderable and made the disclosure real: the URLs are `cdn.paybis.com` / `cdn.sandbox.paybis.com`, so a direct `<img>` would fire one request per method, from the buyer's own IP with a referrer, to a KYC'd payment venue they have not chosen and may never open, on a screen they are only looking at (`CR-TOK-011`; an IP is personal data in the EU, and Paybis otherwise learns nothing about a buyer until `request-id` is minted). Two files, one allow-list. **`src/lib/onramp/methodIconProxy.ts`** is pure and client-importable (added to `serverBoundary.test.ts`'s `CLIENT_ENTRIES`, so it can never be pulled server-side and copied): scheme must be `https:` EXACTLY (`new URL()` parses `javascript:`, `data:` and `file:`, so parseability proves nothing, the same trap `wireIconSchema` documents), hostname matches the two CDN hosts by EXACT string equality (never `endsWith`, which admits `cdn.paybis.com.evil.tld`; never `includes`; never a leading wildcard, which admits `a.cdn.paybis.com`), credentials and an explicit port are refused, a path is required (an allowed host with no path is the CDN ROOT), and the outbound URL is RECONSTRUCTED as `"https://" + <one of two module constants> + <URL-normalised pathname>` so the host fetched is provably one of two literals and the query and fragment are dropped rather than forwarded to a CDN that might honour a redirect parameter. **`src/app/api/onramp/method-icon/route.ts`** re-validates with the same function (the value arrives as a query parameter off the open internet, so the markup's discipline is not a property of the request), then: `redirect: "manual"`, without which an allow-listed host answering `302 http://169.254.169.254/...` is followed by our server from inside the VPC and the allow-list has bought nothing; the content type PINNED to our own constant per matched type and never echoed, so an upstream `text/html` or a smuggled `;` parameter cannot make our origin serve markup; `default-src 'none'; sandbox` + `nosniff` + `Content-Disposition: attachment` because an SVG is a document (attachment is ignored for `<img>` subresources, so the logo still draws, and is decisive for the direct navigation that is the only way a hostile SVG could execute); a 256 KiB cap read from the STREAM rather than trusting `content-length`, with the reader cancelled at the cap and a 5s timeout; and `no-store` on every refusal, since a cacheable failure on a plain GET behind a CDN pins one bad answer in front of every buyer in that POP. Consumed by `PP-DEP-CMP-005`, which degrades a missing, refused or unfetchable icon to ONE neutral tile, identical for every method (a monogram is deliberately NOT used: it would derive from the display name two elements away, and POO-1603 [R4] exists to stop this picker inventing a per-method glyph family). **CSP gains nothing and that is the design:** the proxy is same-origin, so `csp.test.ts` asserts the ABSENCE of any paybis origin in `img-src` (the POO-1451 / POO-1211 inverted-guard pattern), because naming `cdn.paybis.com` there is exactly what a future session would do to "fix" a logo that stopped rendering. **`next/image` + `images.remotePatterns` was named by the issue and cannot serve these logos**, measured against the installed Next 15.5.18: `image-optimizer.js:980` refuses an `image/svg+xml` upstream unless the GLOBAL `dangerouslyAllowSVG` is set, and `:208` throws `Module 'sharp' not found`, with `sharp` absent from package.json and the Dockerfile installing `--ignore-scripts` under `output: "standalone"`; the stale note in `next.config.ts` that sent the first reader down that path is retired in place. Deliberately NOT authenticated: the blast radius is bounded by construction (one of two vendor CDNs, on a path, no query, capped and time-bounded) rather than by a secret, and an HMAC would add an env secret and a deploy coupling to protect public logos. | Lib | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1643](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1643), [POO-1129](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1129) | (code-only; `src/lib/onramp/methodIconProxy.ts`, `src/app/api/onramp/method-icon/route.ts`; epic POO-1129 fiat on-ramp) @rules-v1 |

| `PP-CORE-MCK-008` | Crypto deposit fixture (POO-1624 rules v1): the USDC a crypto deposit "arrives" with on `/deposit`'s invest-context resume, read ONLY behind `isMockMode`. It was `const CRYPTO_RECEIVED = 100` at the top of `DepositScreen.tsx`, three lines above the fiat minimum and indistinguishable from it, and **nothing gated it**: a 2500ms `setTimeout` advanced the crypto path to a receipt and printed the constant as the amount that had landed, in production. Moving it under `src/mocks/` makes the boundary structural rather than a comment, the same move `PP-CORE-MCK-006` made one screen over. $100 matches the screen's own default entry and the $10..$200,000 bound (POO-727) so the harness renders at real scale; under the invest context the shortfall is used instead, so the mock receipt agrees with the banner two screens back (POO-494 [R5]). | `PP-AUTH-LIB-005` | personal_sign encoding (`auth/personalSign.ts`), POO-1407 rules v2. The one place that decides how a message reaches `personal_sign`. EIP-191 takes the payload HEX-ENCODED; every call site in this app passed the raw string, which MetaMask and Rabby accept and Ledger Live does not (it decodes a raw string to an EMPTY buffer and signs THAT, returning a valid signature over nothing). Found in the SIWE handshake, but nothing about it was SIWE-specific: the same raw string went to `personal_sign` from the profile save, both manager writes, the rewards claim and the on-ramp `requestId` mint, so fixing only sign-in would have moved a Ledger user from cannot-sign-in to signed-in-and-every-write-fails, one of them a money path. A shared function rather than the same one-line edit copied six times, because the encoding is a single fact about a single RPC and copies drift | Lib | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1407](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1407) | (code-only; `src/lib/auth/personalSign.ts`) @rules-v2 |

| `PP-STR-MOD-010` | Funding rail rollout across the other five operations (POO-1045 rules v1, hackathon POO-1022: the family-level contract POO-1043's flagship invest does not cover. Withdraw, collect, move-range and close gate at their Review approve, so they `resume()` a transaction that is ALREADY built (the POO-574/596/597 build-then-review handshake), while compound and invest `run()` because neither has a build to resume [R1]. The consequence, and the reason the issue exists: for the four resuming operations provisioning runs AFTER the build, so a funding route measured in minutes can outlive `MAX_BUILT_TX_AGE_MS` and the send would sign a transaction past the server's 5-minute Permit2 `sigDeadline`. `resume()` already rebuilds in that case (POO-885 [R2]'s hard ceiling) and no production path had ever exercised it [R2]. [R3] closes the last silent gap in the gate: the MANAGER's collect was skipped for carrying no `Strategy` object, which is a fact about our data shape and not about what the operation needs, so it now gates off `managed.network` like every other operation gates off its own chain. Op anchors and cancel destinations are unchanged [R4] and the dismissal lock holds for every operation while the route executes [R5]) | Modal | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1045](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1045) | (code-only; `src/features/strategies/components/CollectModal.tsx`; specs `src/features/strategies/components/provisioningRollout.test.tsx` + `src/features/manager/components/provisioningRollout.test.tsx`) @rules-v1 |

This hook is the missing binding. Everything the rail needs from outside is INJECTED rather than imported, for the reason ADR 0003 gives: the three Uniswap calls are `"use server"` actions whose transport reads `UNISWAP_API_KEY`, so passing them as functions keeps the rail out of the server graph and the key out of any bundle it ships in. Mock-safe like every other operation executor in the folder: in mock mode `buildSteps` is `undefined`, the panel falls back to its mock settle, and behaviour is byte-identical to before the issue. **POO-1043 rules v1** closed the two omissions POO-1042 left: the recovery journal is BOUND (POO-1038's idempotency machinery had not run in production at all, so a killed tab mid-bridge had no in-flight record to reconcile), minted at the moment the user approves the route rather than when the plan is quoted, through `createDeferredJournalRecorder` because the rail is built when the plan resolves and the journal is minted a user decision later; and the re-quote confirmer is forwarded, because with none the rail REFUSES a materially worse re-quote rather than signing it, which is the correct default and a dead end for a user who would have accepted the price | Hook | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1042](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1042), [POO-1043](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1043), [POO-1022](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1022) | (code-only; `src/features/strategies/hooks/useProvisioningRail.ts`; consumed by PP-CORE-CMP-046) @rules-v2 |

| `PP-STR-CMP-026` | SwitchNetworkAction (POO-1385 [R6]/[R8] rules v1): the manual "Switch to <Network>" action inside the shared transaction error block, for the two failure kinds where the network IS the problem. The automatic ladder runs before every signature and is right nearly always; it is not right when the wallet needs the user to act first, and a Ledger Live session carries only the networks selected at pairing, so the remedy is enable-then-switch and only the first half lived outside our app. Rendered for `wrongChain` AND `chainUnavailable` on purpose: they differ in what the body copy says to do first and converge on the same next action. Above "Try again", because retrying a chain failure re-runs the build and the quote to arrive back at a switch the user could have made directly. Renders NOTHING without a resolvable supported chain (inherits POO-1026 [R4], never "Switch to undefined") and nothing for every other failure kind, which is its most common behaviour since it sits in the error block of every failed transaction in the app. Emits `wallet_action_blocked` with `block_reason: chain_unavailable`, once per mounted failure and only for the kind that is genuinely blocked: a wallet on the wrong chain is one prompt from proceeding, and counting it would drown the signal. Deliberately NOT used at the `error` phase's `TransactionErrorActions`: that component is shared by five other flows' `TransactionStatus` and returns its details box and its buttons as one Fragment with no seam to pin only the buttons from outside; pinning it means editing a shared component well past this issue's provisioning-only scope, tracked as a fast-follow rather than silently dropped.) | Component | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1525](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1525), [POO-1498](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1498) | (code-only; `src/features/strategies/components/provisioning/StickyActionFooter.tsx`; Storybook `Strategies/Provisioning/StickyActionFooter`) @rules-v1 |
| `PP-STR-CMP-028` | OnRampMethodStep (POO-1129 rules v1 · POO-1576 rules v1 · POO-1618 rules v1, epic POO-1498): the "Choose how to pay" step between the buy amount and the Paybis checkout. The provisioning buy route NEVER asked before this: `resolveWidgetPrefill` called `pickDefaultPaymentMethod`, which prefers a card by regex, and that method was sent to the mint, so a buyer who wanted a bank transfer, SEPA or Pix had no way to say so and the charge the funding picker printed belonged to a method they had not chosen. Rows carry the provider's `displayName`, its `labels` as neutral chips (POO-1603 [R3]: rendered, never branched on), and either the row's own charge for this order or `Min. $X` where that charge would be. **POO-1129 D1 cancelled the `Best price` pill** (Murilo: "use as labels do vendor so"): no superlative of ours sits beside a money figure, the chip slot is the vendor's alone, and `funding_method_best_price` came out with it (a PARAM on `funding_method_chosen`, never an event, so no census count moved). `bestPrice.ts` (`PP-CORE-LIB-100`) went back to unwired at D1 and is DELETED by POO-1639: the cheapest-viable default it was kept for was never built and nothing schedules it, so the comparison had no caller and no owner. **POO-1129 D2/D3: a row whose own charge is below its own floor BLOCKS** and the charge is never raised. It renders in its own position at reduced emphasis, never hidden and never reordered, carrying the refusal INSTEAD of its minimum, keeping its radio, focusable, `aria-disabled` and never `disabled` (a `disabled` control leaves the tab order, so a screen-reader user would never learn why the option does not serve), and it can never become the value the mint receives. This is `/deposit`'s shipped semantics (PR #897, `PP-DEP-MOD-001`) applied to the same rows, because POO-1609 AC6 requires the two screens to agree for the same method and the same order: POO-1609's TIER 1 comparison and only tier 1. A row the provider merely did not PRICE is a different thing, stays selectable and names the floor as the PROVIDER's once picked, because an absent charge is not a comparison and the decided fail direction is that a method wrongly blocked removes an option the buyer could have used. When EVERY row is blocked the step draws the dead end rather than letting it happen (POO-1609 [P38] / AC8, which that hotfix deferred to the picker rebuild): `Continue` is disabled and `method.allBlocked` names the lowest floor, with `Cancel` as the exit to the step the amount lives on. `/deposit` cannot reach that state at all, because its PINNED quote accumulates refusals one at a time and the fallback it hands back is always the CHECKED row, which dispatches no change event and so can never become the next refusal; this step reads the UNPINNED listing quote (POO-1599), which prices every row at once. **POO-1129 A7-b: non-instant methods are OUT of the first iteration**, filtered by `selectInstantMethods` upstream of every row, which contradicts the frames (they draw `Bank transfer` here) and the rule wins. It FAILS OPEN: the captured vocabulary is all positive, so "not instant" is an absence rather than an assertion, and a set carrying no `instant` at all shows every method and reports `onramp.instant_filter_empty` rather than emptying the step. **POO-1618 rules v1** makes the currency a real control (Rafael 2026-08-14: "none of them needs to be read-only"): a native `<select>` over the supported set POO-1621 now publishes (`useOnRampCurrencies`, `PP-CORE-HOK-033`), whose value is always the currency the SERVER echoed rather than the buyer's request, so a refused proposal can never print one currency beside another's figures. Changing it re-lists the methods, because the SET is a function of the currency and not a relabelling of it (`directa24_pix` is BRL-only, `poolparty-trustly` is USD-only), and the reload renders a skeleton (`loading`, from `useBuyRouteQuote.methodsLoading`) rather than either the previous currency's rows (the POO-1513 stale-figure class) or the empty state (which would blame the provider for our own round trip). With no readable set there is no control and the step states the resolved currency exactly as before, because a Select offering what it cannot switch to lies (POO-494 [R1]). An empty list informs and still continues on the shipped prefill (POO-1576 Q3), never a dead end and never a silent skip. Hosted by `ProvisioningPanel` (`PP-CORE-CMP-046`), which owns the funnel events. | Component | Figma `3c`/`3d`/`3e`/`3g` (approximated per Rafael 2026-08-14: no flag assets, no vendor logos, pixel fidelity is a follow-up) | In Review | [POO-1576](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1576), [POO-1618](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1618), [POO-1621](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1621), [POO-1498](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1498) | (code-only; `src/features/strategies/components/provisioning/OnRampMethodStep.tsx`; Storybook `Strategies/Provisioning/OnRampMethodStep`; keys `provisioning.onramp.method.*`, all 12 locales) @rules-v2 |
| `PP-STR-CMP-029` | Privy buy step (`src/features/strategies/components/provisioning/PrivyBuyStep.tsx`), POO-1808 rules v1, epic POO-1793. The provisioning buy leg on the second rail, rendered in the slot `PaybisWidgetFrame` occupies for the first one and settling the same `runOnRampBuy` promise, so `buildOnRampSteps`, `ProvisioningOrder`, `sizeFromRealBalance` and `useProvisioningRail` are NOT modified. Split in two on purpose: the logic takes its adapter, watcher and probe as PROPS and a thin wrapper binds the hooks, which is what lets the suite drive an inconclusive exit, a ceiling and a refusal without mocking `@privy-io/react-auth` in a component test. **[R3]** it resolves on the observed DELTA and never on the provider's claim; at the visible ceiling it HANDS OVER to the panel's settling screen (`ONRAMP_SETTLING_CODE`, shared with the Paybis rail), never called cancelled, so the buyer reads a pause instead of waiting on a promise nobody will settle (cross-session resumption is POO-1833). **[R4]** a `"ETH-BASE"` order is refused before opening (`ONRAMP_NATIVE_UNAVAILABLE`), because POO-1820 answered that native is not for sale on this rail; the paymaster-versus-disclosure decision is Murilo's and this refusal is the honest placeholder. **[R5]** the destination balance is read once on mount and the CTA refuses until it exists, because a baseline taken after the buyer may already have paid subtracts their own money from the delta. **[R6]** coverage is asked before opening: `uncovered` refuses, `unknown` and `amount-too-low` do not block. **[R7]** the checkout opens in the SERVER-resolved buyer currency (threaded from `buyRouteQuote.currencyCodeFrom`, never `order.fiatCurrency`, which the sizer writes as a constant "USD"); a resolved currency the rail cannot charge in is REFUSED rather than silently defaulted to dollars, USD is used only where nothing resolved, the currency list is never narrowed, and the USD prefill rides only when the buyer pays in dollars. **[R8]** all four refusals report `tx_amount_blocked` with the panel's `flow` and `strategy_id`. | Component | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1808](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1808), [POO-1793](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1793) | (code-only; `PrivyBuyStep.tsx`, `PrivyBuyStep.stories.tsx`, `PrivyBuyStep.test.tsx`, `ProvisioningPanel.privyBuy.test.tsx`, `ProvisioningPanel.privyBuy.analytics.test.tsx`) @rules-v1 |
| `PP-STR-LIB-022` | onRampMethodRows (POO-1129 rules v1 · POO-1576 rules v1): the row rules of the "Choose how to pay" step, pure and React-free so they are pinned by a test rather than by a screenshot, and so the two surfaces that print these rows cannot disagree about what one MEANS. Beyond eligibility the list is UNFILTERED and in the provider's own order; a charge is matched BY ID and never by position (POO-1413); and "unpriced" is the VENDOR's fact (`paymentMethodErrors`, POO-1599), never inferred by comparing a minimum from one call against a charge from another. A quote that priced NOTHING marks no row unpriced, so the gas-first leg does not render every method as refused. **POO-1129 D1:** no `Best price` field and no call to `selectBestPriceMethodIds` — the pill is cancelled, so a module that computes a superlative is no longer wired to a row that prints one. **POO-1129 D2/D3 [R7]:** `blocked` is POO-1609's TIER 1 and only tier 1, matching what `/deposit` shipped in PR #897 so AC6 holds — row R's own charge against row R's own floor, failing OPEN on an absent charge, an absent floor, a zero on either side, or two currency codes that are not provably equal (POO-333: there is no FX source, and a method wrongly blocked removes an option the buyer could have used while a method wrongly offered is recoverable at checkout). `>` and never `>=`, because a floor is a minimum the vendor accepts. Tier 2 (the entered amount against an UNPRICED row's floor) is deliberately absent on BOTH screens; closing it belongs in one change across both. **POO-1129 A7-b [R8]:** `selectInstantMethods` is the eligibility half, kept strictly separate from presentation: [R5] keeps a ROW value-blind, while eligibility reads a label's VALUE, which the redesign brief (§4.2, line 104) forbids of the UI. That is a knowing, temporary exception carrying a `PP-DEBT(SEV:MED) POO-1606`, not a reconciliation — this is the one place a label's VALUE is read, and it answers "may this method be offered" rather than "how does this row look". It FAILS OPEN and says so with `failedOpen`, because the captured vocabulary (`instant`, `low-fee`, `high-approval-rate`) is entirely positive, so a set with no `instant` is far more likely an uncaptured vocabulary than a world with no instant rail, and a filter that matches nothing is worse than no filter. **POO-1632 converges the duplicated helpers rather than the builders.** Two of the issue's three findings had already dissolved: `src/features/deposit/lib/paymentMethodRows.ts`, the rival builder it opens on, DOES NOT EXIST (`/deposit` has no separate builder), and the "three `methodToken` trim helpers" were two, the second going with `bestPrice.ts` (POO-1639). What was live is the CURRENCY guard: this file kept a private `sameFiat` whose own comment named `methodFloor.ts` as the shared home it was waiting on, `methodFloor.ts` landed as PP-CORE-LIB-101, and the comment went stale, so one money surface carried two definitions of "same currency" that disagreed on the blank pair. `sameFiat` is now exported from PP-CORE-LIB-101 and imported here, on this file's STRICTER semantics (a blank code names no currency and never matches), which is a no-op for both call sites and closes the `/deposit` one against a payload with both codes empty. Still open on POO-1632, measured not assumed: the three hand-maintained transcriptions of the Paybis payload. The real captures (PP-CORE-MCK-007) would strengthen the TEST fixtures, since they OBSERVE both the tie ([R7]'s, 3 methods at one charge in 7 of 13 quote records) and the blocked row (`pool-party-manual-bank-transfer` priced at EUR 10.68 against its own EUR 200.00 floor) that we currently compose; the blocker is that the captures carry the real `pool-party-*` ids against the transcriptions' older `poolparty-*`, so every id-naming assertion in four test files and one story moves at once, and `onRampPaymentMethods.ts` is not a transcription but three chosen methods plus a rate-and-fixed charge MODEL that answers for any amount the harness types, which a one-order-size snapshot cannot replace. The two screens still compute BLOCKED differently (this file is tier 1 only, `PaymentMethodList` calls the two-tiered `isMethodBelowFloor`), which is a stated behaviour difference and a rules decision, not a duplication. | Lib | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1576](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1576), [POO-1632](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1632), [POO-1666](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1666) | (code-only; `src/features/strategies/lib/onRampMethodRows.ts`) @rules-v3 |
| `PP-STR-LIB-023` | Buffer ledger (`src/features/strategies/lib/bufferLedger.ts`: `measureBufferAsk`), POO-1811 rules v1, epic POO-1793. Measures the price-move buffer before anyone argues about its size. `SEED_BUFFER_RATE` has been 5% since it was written and one of the three components its comment names has no code behind it (`costBreakdown.ts` excludes swap cost because the quote is already net of it), yet nothing was ever recorded: the shipped `consumeBuffer` returned a boolean and kept no trace of what it was asked. This returns the DECISION plus a measurement `{askedBps, cumulativeBps, budgetBps, headroomBps, held, askIndex}`, which `ProvisioningPanel` emits as `funding_buffer_consumed` once per ask, held or refused. **[R2] it cannot influence sizing**: the decision is byte-identical to the shipped callback (pinned against a transcription of it across the whole range, including the exact boundary and the flagship suite's 1030 bps refusal), the module is pure with the running total left in the panel's ref, and a test asserts it imports nothing that sizes. **[R3]** the rate stays `0.05`, asserted; reducing it to 3% is P4-3's decision and is blocked on the evidence this produces. | Lib | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1811](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1811), [POO-1793](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1793) | (code-only; `src/features/strategies/lib/bufferLedger.ts`, `src/features/strategies/lib/bufferLedger.test.ts`) @rules-v1 |
| `PP-STR-HOK-022` | useStickyFooterShadow (POO-1525 rules v1, epic POO-1498: whether `StickyActionFooter`'s elevation shadow should show, i.e. whether ITS OWN scrolling ancestor still has content below the visible viewport. Walks up from the footer's node to the nearest ancestor whose computed `overflow-y` is `auto`/`scroll` (`Dialog.tsx`/`Sheet.tsx` already own that on the element every host mounts `ProvisioningPanel` inside, confirmed against both today's `DialogContent` and the `SheetContent` `#856` introduces), then tracks `scrollHeight - scrollTop - clientHeight > 1` via a scroll listener plus a `ResizeObserver`/`MutationObserver` pair on the container, the two sources that move the metrics with no scroll event ever firing: the viewport resizing, the content growing or shrinking underneath. Degrades to `false` (no shadow) whenever nothing is measurable, no scroll ancestor found or bare jsdom's `scrollHeight`/`clientHeight` both `0`, the same posture `useVirtualizeGate`'s `hasLayout` documents for an unmeasurable state: never a guess.) | Hook | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1525](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1525), [POO-1498](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1498) | (code-only; `src/features/strategies/components/provisioning/useStickyFooterShadow.ts`; consumed by PP-STR-CMP-027) @rules-v1 |

| `PP-DEP-CMP-005` | PaymentMethodList (payment-method rows shared by `PaymentMethodDialog`, below `lg`, and the `lg`+ inline panel section, so the blocked-row and every-row-charge rules are defined once). **POO-1609 rules v2:** a row below its provider's floor renders BLOCKED in its own position, never hidden and never reordered, radio kept, `aria-disabled="true"` and never `disabled` (a disabled control leaves the tab order, so a screen-reader user never learns why the option does not serve). Every row shows its OWN charge when the quote priced it (POO-1612, POO-1599). **D1 (epic POO-1129, 16/08): the Best price pill is DELETED**, not hidden — no superlative of ours sits beside a money figure, and the chip slot belongs to the vendor. **POO-1603 [R3]:** that slot now prints the vendor's own `labels`, all of them, verbatim, untranslated, in one generic chip that never branches on the value; **POO-1643:** `icon` now RENDERS, and still fires no request at Paybis: every `src` is a relative path to our own proxy (`PP-CORE-SEC-003`) and the server makes the vendor call, so X11's constraint is met by proxying rather than by omission. A missing, refused or unfetchable icon degrades to ONE neutral tile, identical for every method and recovering from a runtime failure via `onError` (the `ManagerAvatar` pattern, PP-MGR-CMP-033); a monogram is deliberately NOT used, because it would derive from the display name sitting two elements away and POO-1603 [R4] exists to stop this picker inventing a per-method glyph family · `src/features/deposit/components/PaymentMethodList.tsx` | Component | Done | Done | [POO-1612](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1612), [POO-1609](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1609), [POO-1603](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1603), [POO-1129](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1129), [POO-1643](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1643) | (code-only) @rules-v4 |
| `PP-DEP-CMP-006` | CurrencySelect (names the resolved on-ramp currency above the payment-method section, real data in both modes via `Intl.DisplayNames`; the interactive browse-44 control is mock-only, gated on `options`/`onSelect` both being passed, never by accident). No flags: the two-letter-code trick breaks on `XAF`, and this ships `USD · US dollar` rather than derive one. The visual/interaction pattern mirrors `FilterDropdown` (`PP-CORE-CMP-059`) rather than reusing it directly (kept feature-scoped given the alpha's timeline; a shared searchable-Select primitive is a reasonable follow-up). Note: POO-1613's own text cites a non-existent `PP-CORE-CMP-049 · Select` — that id belongs to `AprTooltip`, unrelated; flagged as a drafting error, not resolved here · `src/features/deposit/components/CurrencySelect.tsx` | Component | Done | Done | [POO-1613](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1613) | (code-only) |
| `PP-DEP-CMP-007` | DepositPrivyCheckout (`src/features/deposit/components/DepositPrivyCheckout.tsx`), POO-1807 rules v1, epic POO-1793. The `/deposit` half of the Privy rail: plugs into the `onramp` step the way `StandaloneOnRampRail` (`PP-DEP-CMP-004`) does, owns the ADR-0006 observation window and the provider-neutral opening screen, and hands every outcome back to `PP-DEP-SCR-001` through one callback each (`onSettled` / `onSettling` / `onUnverified` / `onFailed`). The checkout promise is a PROP rather than something this component opens, and that is structural: MoonPay, Coinbase and Meld open a top-level popup, which a browser allows only from inside the click's own synchronous turn, so a mount effect would be blocked and the buyer would see nothing. The host therefore opens it in the click handler and this component owns everything after. Same reasoning puts the baseline read in the host. A hard `no` is the ONLY outcome it reports as a failure: `confirmed` and `maybe` both enter the settling window, because after 3.40.0 a charged card and an abandonment are indistinguishable at the exit. Never names the provider on screen (`deposit.onramp.checkout.*`, "secure checkout": no vendor in the values AND none in the key path), and emits nothing: the funnel belongs to the screen that owns the step machine | Component | n/a (code) | In Review | [POO-1807](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1807), [POO-1793](https://linear.app/yeildbay/issue/POO-1793) | (code-only; `src/features/deposit/components/DepositPrivyCheckout.tsx`) @rules-v1 |

## TOOLS · Developer tooling (Uniswap v4 hook risk scan)

> New area, opened 2026-09-13 for the hackathon Tools page. No Figma: this is a developer surface, specced from `hookrisk/docs/hackathon/DEMO_RUNBOOK.md` rather than from a design frame, so every row is `n/a (code)` on Design. Gated by the `hookTools` flag (`docs/FEATURE_FLAGS.md`), which is also the removal seam if the page never leaves the fork. `API` is a new type token: a Next route handler (see `02_NAMING_CONVENTION.md`).

| ID | Title | Type | Design | Impl | Linear | Figma |
|----|-------|------|--------|------|--------|-------|
| `PP-TOOLS-SCR-001` | Tools page at `/tools` (flag-guarded route + loading skeleton) | Screen | n/a (code) | Done |  | (code-only) |
| `PP-TOOLS-CMP-001` | HookRiskScreen (form, scan status, report; owns the `tools_hookrisk_*` funnel) | Component | n/a (code) | Done |  | (code-only) |
| `PP-TOOLS-CMP-002` | MarkdownReport (renders `HOOK_RISK.md`, raw HTML off) | Component | n/a (code) | Done |  | (code-only) |
| `PP-TOOLS-API-001` | `/api/tools/hookrisk` (POST starts a scan, GET polls it; Node runtime) | API route | n/a (code) | Done |  | (code-only) |
| `PP-TOOLS-LIB-001` | hookrisk cache paths and TTL (cache key, hashed job dir, 24 h sweep) | Lib | n/a (code) | Done |  | (code-only) |
| `PP-TOOLS-LIB-002` | Verified-source fetch (Etherscan V2 `getsourcecode`, three `SourceCode` shapes) | Lib | n/a (code) | Done |  | (code-only) |
| `PP-TOOLS-LIB-003` | Foundry project materialisation (foundry.toml, remappings, path sanitisation) | Lib | n/a (code) | Done |  | (code-only) |
| `PP-TOOLS-LIB-004` | hookrisk toolchain preflight and invocation (exit codes, `spawn` seam) | Lib | n/a (code) | Done |  | (code-only) |
| `PP-TOOLS-LIB-005` | hookrisk scan jobs (in-memory registry, one job per cache key) | Lib | n/a (code) | Done |  | (code-only) |
| `PP-TOOLS-LIB-006` | Scan contract shared by client and server (chains, job snapshot) | Lib | n/a (code) | Done |  | (code-only) |

> **The scan runs the real toolchain or it fails loudly.** There is no mock branch: a fabricated risk report is worse than no page. Without `forge`, `slither`, a built `hookrisk/cli/dist/cli.js` or `ETHERSCAN_API_KEY`, the job fails fast naming exactly what is missing, and the screen prints that instead of a report.
